    *   **Focus on Sentence-Level Translation:** The strict focus on sentence-level translation, while providing clarity, omits a broader discussion of document-level translation or multimodal applications where LLMs could significantly impact contextual understanding. This scope limitation could miss important emerging trends.

*   **Potential Influence:** This paper can serve as a valuable starting point for new researchers in LLM-based MT, guiding them toward the most relevant literature and open research questions. Practitioners can use it to select appropriate techniques for their specific MT tasks and constraints. Overall, the survey promotes further exploration and development in this rapidly evolving field.

**Score: 7**

**Justification:** The survey offers a valuable and well-structured overview of LLMs in machine translation. It synthesizes recent advancements, identifies key challenges, and points to future research directions. The survey's comprehensiveness, practicality, and balanced perspective contribute to its significance. However, the lack of direct cross-paper comparisons and limited analysis of the underlying mechanisms and contextual translation constrain its overall impact, preventing a higher score. Therefore a score of 7 balances the current state of the research in LM based MT and it's limitations as a static snapshot in a field developing at such a rapid rate.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Is the Reversal Curse a Binding Problem? Uncovering Limitations of Transformers from a Basic Generalization Failure
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.01928v1)
- **Authors**: Boshi Wang, Huan Sun
- **Abstract**: Despite their impressive capabilities, LLMs exhibit a basic generalization failure known as the Reversal Curse, where they struggle to learn reversible factual associations. Understanding why this occurs could help identify weaknesses in current models and advance their generalization and robustness. In this paper, we conjecture that the Reversal Curse in LLMs is a manifestation of the long-standing binding problem in cognitive science, neuroscience and AI. Specifically, we identify two primary causes of the Reversal Curse stemming from transformers' limitations in conceptual binding: the inconsistency and entanglements of concept representations. We perform a series of experiments that support these conjectures. Our exploration leads to a model design based on JEPA (Joint-Embedding Predictive Architecture) that for the first time breaks the Reversal Curse without side-stepping it with specialized data augmentation or non-causal masking, and moreover, generalization could be further improved by incorporating special memory layers that support disentangled concept representations. We demonstrate that the skill of reversal unlocks a new kind of memory integration that enables models to solve large-scale arithmetic reasoning problems via parametric forward-chaining, outperforming frontier LLMs based on non-parametric memory and prolonged explicit reasoning.
- **Summary**: The paper "Is the Reversal Curse a Binding Problem? Uncovering Limitations of Transformers from a Basic Generalization Failure" investigates the Reversal Curse in Large Language Models (LLMs), attributing it to the binding problem, specifically the inconsistency and entanglement of concept representations within transformers. The paper proposes that transformers struggle to consistently represent concepts across different contexts and lack inductive biases to decouple abstract concepts from direct perceptions. The authors demonstrate that transformers can learn reversal when inputs are represented at an abstract concept level. They propose a model based on JEPA (Joint-Embedding Predictive Architecture) to address the inconsistency and entanglement. They also use memory layers to further improve generalization. The paper showcases the potential of this design for large-scale arithmetic reasoning problems, outperforming frontier LLMs.

**Critical Evaluation of Novelty and Significance:**

The paper presents a fairly novel perspective on the Reversal Curse by framing it within the context of the binding problem. The identification of inconsistency and entanglement as key factors contributing to the curse is valuable. The experimental demonstration that transformers can learn reversal at the concept level is a significant finding, highlighting the importance of representation. The proposed JEPA-based architecture and use of memory layers provide practical solutions for mitigating the curse, and the results on arithmetic reasoning showcase the potential of these approaches for complex reasoning tasks. The linking of the reversal curse to fundamental cognitive limitations is valuable.

However, there are some aspects of the paper that limits its novelty and significance:

1.  **Incremental Contributions:** While framing the Reversal Curse as a binding problem is interesting, the proposed solutions of JEPA-based architecture and memory layers are not entirely novel architectural innovations in the broader context of NLP and computer vision. A better comparison to other related methods (e.g. those addressing knowledge editing or variable binding) is warranted.
2.  **Task-Specific Design:** The proposed architecture is heavily tailored for the Reversal Curse and arithmetic reasoning tasks. The degree to which the architectural choices and specific JEPA implementation are generalizable requires more exploration and experimental validation on other datasets or tasks. The benefits from "breaking the curse" may also be overstated.
3.  **Limited theoretical analysis:** While the paper introduces a theoretical perspective, it is not heavily grounded in theory. A more extensive theoretical framework would greatly enhance the paper's impact.
4. The limitations section does not address the computational overhead of introducing memory layers.

Despite these limitations, the paper makes a valuable contribution by offering a new perspective on an important problem in LLMs and demonstrating the potential of specific architectures to improve reasoning abilities. Overall, I assign a score of 6/10, reflecting the paper's valuable insights and practical solutions tempered by the task-specific nature of the proposed architecture, and the limited incremental contribution to the field in terms of methodology and analysis.

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### A thorough benchmark of automatic text classification: From traditional approaches to large language models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.01930v1)
- **Authors**: Washington Cunha, Leonardo Rocha, Marcos André Gonçalves
- **Abstract**: Automatic text classification (ATC) has experienced remarkable advancements in the past decade, best exemplified by recent small and large language models (SLMs and LLMs), leveraged by Transformer architectures. Despite recent effectiveness improvements, a comprehensive cost-benefit analysis investigating whether the effectiveness gains of these recent approaches compensate their much higher costs when compared to more traditional text classification approaches such as SVMs and Logistic Regression is still missing in the literature. In this context, this work's main contributions are twofold: (i) we provide a scientifically sound comparative analysis of the cost-benefit of twelve traditional and recent ATC solutions including five open LLMs, and (ii) a large benchmark comprising {22 datasets}, including sentiment analysis and topic classification, with their (train-validation-test) partitions based on folded cross-validation procedures, along with documentation, and code. The release of code, data, and documentation enables the community to replicate experiments and advance the field in a more scientifically sound manner. Our comparative experimental results indicate that LLMs outperform traditional approaches (up to 26%-7.1% on average) and SLMs (up to 4.9%-1.9% on average) in terms of effectiveness. However, LLMs incur significantly higher computational costs due to fine-tuning, being, on average 590x and 8.5x slower than traditional methods and SLMs, respectively. Results suggests the following recommendations: (1) LLMs for applications that require the best possible effectiveness and can afford the costs; (2) traditional methods such as Logistic Regression and SVM for resource-limited applications or those that cannot afford the cost of tuning large LLMs; and (3) SLMs like Roberta for near-optimal effectiveness-efficiency trade-off.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

This paper presents a comprehensive benchmark of automatic text classification (ATC) methods, comparing traditional approaches (SVM, Logistic Regression, Random Forests) with Small Language Models (SLMs - RoBERTa, BERT, BART, XLNet) and Large Language Models (LLMs - DeepSeek, LLaMA, Mistral, BloomZ). The authors evaluate these models across 22 datasets covering topic classification and sentiment analysis. The study investigates both the effectiveness (Macro-F1 score) and the computational costs (runtime and estimated carbon emissions) associated with each method. The results indicate that while LLMs generally outperform traditional methods and SLMs in effectiveness, they come at a significantly higher computational cost. The paper provides recommendations for model selection based on the specific application's needs: LLMs for maximum effectiveness when costs are not a constraint, traditional methods for resource-limited applications, and SLMs for a trade-off between effectiveness and efficiency.  The code, datasets, and documentation are publicly released for reproducibility and further research.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its comprehensive and scientifically rigorous approach to benchmarking a wide range of ATC methods, encompassing traditional techniques, SLMs, and LLMs. While previous studies have compared some of these models, this paper stands out for its systematic cost-benefit analysis, considering computational resources and carbon emissions in addition to effectiveness. Also, releasing the code, data, and partitions significantly adds to the reproducibility and advancement of the area. The inclusion of multiple open-source LLMs and a detailed analysis using open-source tools is a noteworthy contribution.
*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial gap in the literature by providing a cost-effectiveness perspective on the adoption of recent LLMs for ATC. The increasing popularity of LLMs often overshadows the practical considerations of computational resources, environmental impact, and whether the gains in effectiveness justify the increased costs. This benchmark helps practitioners make informed decisions about model selection based on their specific constraints and requirements. By providing a solid experimental setup and releasing artifacts, the work also enables the community to build upon these results and explore new avenues of research. The comparison across a variety of datasets is also significant, since it highlights the generalization performance of each method across tasks. The explicit mentioning of the need for more environmentally conscious approaches is also relevant.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Benchmark:** The paper covers a wide range of models and datasets.
    *   **Cost-Benefit Analysis:** It goes beyond just accuracy, incorporating runtime and carbon emissions.
    *   **Reproducibility:** The public release of code and data greatly enhances its value.
    *   **Practical Recommendations:** It provides actionable insights for practitioners.
    *   **Rigorous Methodology:** The use of cross-validation and statistical significance tests ensures the reliability of the results.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Hyperparameter Tuning:** While the paper mentions hyperparameter tuning, more details could be provided on the specific search strategies used for different models. Addressing this further would bolster the results and eliminate potential concerns over the fairness of the comparisons.  While the paper mentions using Hydra, expanding on why those specific ranges and methods were chosen would be beneficial.
    *   **Limited Model Variations:** The analysis could benefit from exploring variations within each model category. For example, different sizes of LLMs within the LLaMA family could be compared to assess the trade-off between model size, effectiveness, and cost.
    *   **Static Datasets:** The datasets, while varied, are static. Exploring the models' robustness to distribution shifts over time, especially for sentiment analysis, could provide valuable insights.
*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to become a standard reference for researchers and practitioners working on ATC. Its comprehensive methodology and open-source nature will encourage further investigation into the cost-effectiveness of different models and the development of more efficient and sustainable ATC solutions. It also highlights how important it is to not only push the state of the art on benchmark datasets, but to also carefully choose a model/method suitable for the environment/hardware it will be deployed on.
* **Concerns:** The paper has not considered a Zero-shot learning scenario for LLMs.  That evaluation is crucial to assess their performance without any task-specific training, further informing the debate on their utility.
*   **Score Rationale:** While the paper has some minor weaknesses, its strengths significantly outweigh them. The comprehensive and rigorous methodology, the valuable cost-benefit analysis, and the commitment to reproducibility make it a significant contribution to the field of ATC. The practical recommendations and potential influence on future research justify a high score. Considering these points and the need for the aforementioned addition of a Zero-shot learning section, the paper deserves a score of 8. The novelty in evaluating ATC goes beyond just accuracy, incorporating runtime and environmental concerns.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### ILLUME+: Illuminating Unified MLLM with Dual Visual Tokenization and Diffusion Refinement
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.01934v2)
- **Authors**: Runhui Huang, Chunwei Wang, Junwei Yang, Guansong Lu, Yunlong Yuan, Jianhua Han, Lu Hou, Wei Zhang, Lanqing Hong, Hengshuang Zhao, Hang Xu
- **Abstract**: We present ILLUME+ that leverages dual visual tokenization and a diffusion decoder to improve both deep semantic understanding and high-fidelity image generation. Existing unified models have struggled to simultaneously handle the three fundamental capabilities in a unified model: understanding, generation, and editing. Models like Chameleon and EMU3 utilize VQGAN for image discretization, due to the lack of deep semantic interaction, they lag behind specialist models like LLaVA in visual understanding tasks. To mitigate this, LaViT and ILLUME employ semantic encoders for tokenization, but they struggle with image editing due to poor texture preservation. Meanwhile, Janus series decouples the input and output image representation, limiting their abilities to seamlessly handle interleaved image-text understanding and generation. In contrast, ILLUME+ introduces a unified dual visual tokenizer, DualViTok, which preserves both fine-grained textures and text-aligned semantics while enabling a coarse-to-fine image representation strategy for multimodal understanding and generation. Additionally, we employ a diffusion model as the image detokenizer for enhanced generation quality and efficient super-resolution. ILLUME+ follows a continuous-input, discrete-output scheme within the unified MLLM and adopts a progressive training procedure that supports dynamic resolution across the vision tokenizer, MLLM, and diffusion decoder. This design allows for flexible and efficient context-aware image editing and generation across diverse tasks. ILLUME+ (3B) exhibits competitive performance against existing unified MLLMs and specialized models across multimodal understanding, generation, and editing benchmarks. With its strong performance, ILLUME+ provides a scalable and versatile foundation for future multimodal applications. Project Page: https://illume-unified-mllm.github.io/.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the ILLUME+ paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ILLUME+, an improved version of the ILLUME model, designed to enhance both deep semantic understanding and high-fidelity image generation in Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs). ILLUME+ utilizes a dual visual tokenizer (DualViTok) to preserve both fine-grained textures and text-aligned semantics, enabling a coarse-to-fine image representation strategy. Additionally, a diffusion model is employed as an image detokenizer to improve generation quality and enable efficient super-resolution.  The model employs a progressive training procedure that supports dynamic resolution across its components. ILLUME+ demonstrates competitive performance across various multimodal understanding, generation, and editing benchmarks, supporting flexible high-resolution images and detailed image synthesis.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents incremental but valuable improvements upon the existing ILLUME architecture. The DualViTok is a novel component, designed to address the limitations of existing approaches that either compromise semantic understanding or texture preservation in image editing tasks. The integration of a diffusion model for detokenization and super-resolution is a significant contribution, enhancing the quality of generated images and enabling efficient processing of high-resolution content. The dynamic resolution training is also a valuable contribution, improving training stability.

*   **Significance:** The research contributes to the ongoing efforts in developing unified MLLMs capable of handling both visual understanding and generation tasks effectively. The enhanced image editing capabilities of ILLUME+, particularly the improved texture preservation, are significant. The model's ability to handle flexible high-resolution images enhances its practical applicability in various domains. It shows promising results against models specialized for understanding or generation, showing its potential in various multimodal tasks.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The dual visual tokenizer addresses a key challenge in unified MLLMs.
    *   The use of a diffusion model for detokenization enhances image quality.
    *   The model demonstrates strong performance across various benchmarks.
    *   The architecture supports flexible resolutions and efficient super-resolution.
    *   Comprehensive ablations studies give insight into the design choices.
    *   The paper is clear and well-written, with a good overview of related work.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The reliance on a pre-trained vision encoder (QwenViT) might limit the model's ability to learn completely novel visual representations.
    *   While competitive, the performance gains over existing unified models are not always substantial. A more in-depth analysis comparing the trade-offs in various tasks could be beneficial.
    *   The paper relies heavily on existing components, like diffusion models and VQGAN architectures. While the integration is innovative, the core components are not entirely new.

*   **Potential Influence:** The ILLUME+ architecture provides a valuable framework for future research in unified MLLMs. The DualViTok and diffusion-based detokenization approach could inspire new designs for visual tokenizers and image generation methods. The model's ability to handle high-resolution images effectively makes it relevant to various applications, including document processing, image editing, and content creation.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a significant contribution to the field of unified MLLMs by addressing the limitations of existing approaches and introducing innovative components. While the model builds upon existing work, the combination of the dual visual tokenizer, diffusion-based detokenization, and progressive training pipeline is novel and achieves promising results. However, the incremental nature of the improvements and the reliance on existing components, as well as the fact that it relies on pre-trained models and does not introduce a new training scheme for those from scratch, prevent it from receiving a higher score.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Critical Thinking: Which Kinds of Complexity Govern Optimal Reasoning Length?
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.01935v1)
- **Authors**: Celine Lee, Alexander M. Rush, Keyon Vafa
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) often benefit from verbalized reasoning at inference time, but it remains unclear which aspects of task difficulty these extra reasoning tokens address. To investigate this question, we formalize a framework using deterministic finite automata (DFAs). DFAs offer a formalism through which we can characterize task complexity through measurable properties such as run length (number of reasoning steps required) and state-space size (decision complexity). We first show that across different tasks and models of different sizes and training paradigms, there exists an optimal amount of reasoning tokens such that the probability of producing a correct solution is maximized. We then investigate which properties of complexity govern this critical length: we find that task instances with longer corresponding underlying DFA runs (i.e. demand greater latent state-tracking requirements) correlate with longer reasoning lengths, but, surprisingly, that DFA size (i.e. state-space complexity) does not. We then demonstrate an implication of these findings: being able to predict the optimal number of reasoning tokens for new problems and filtering out non-optimal length answers results in consistent accuracy improvements.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates how task complexity influences the optimal length of reasoning chains generated by Large Language Models (LLMs). The authors introduce a framework using Deterministic Finite Automata (DFAs) to characterize task complexity through measurable properties like run length (number of reasoning steps) and state-space size (decision complexity).  They empirically demonstrate that there's an optimal number of reasoning tokens (the "critical length") that maximizes solution accuracy, and this length varies across tasks and models.  The study finds that longer DFA runs (greater latent state-tracking requirements) correlate with longer optimal reasoning lengths, but DFA size (state-space complexity) does not significantly correlate. Finally, they show that predicting the optimal number of reasoning tokens and filtering out answers with non-optimal lengths can improve accuracy.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The use of DFAs to formally characterize and manipulate task complexity in the context of LLM reasoning is a significant strength. While prior work has explored optimal reasoning length, this paper provides a more principled, theoretically grounded approach for analyzing it. The finding that run length is more impactful than state-space size is counter-intuitive and potentially significant. The idea of rejection sampling based on predicted critical length adds a practical application. This seems incremental but solid, contributing to a growing understanding of reasoning strategies in LLMs.

*   **Significance:** The paper's findings have several potentially important implications:
    *   **Optimizing LLM Inference:** Understanding what drives the need for longer reasoning chains allows for more efficient use of LLM inference compute, avoiding wasteful "overthinking".
    *   **Improving Accuracy:**  The rejection sampling technique, even with a simple prediction model, demonstrates a path towards improving LLM accuracy at inference time.
    *   **Understanding Implicit Reasoning:**  The finding regarding run length versus state-space size provides insight into the type of implicit computation LLMs might be performing when reasoning.  The study pushes beyond simply observing performance gains from more tokens to actually investigating why those tokens are helpful.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The formalization using DFAs is well-motivated and executed.
    *   The experimental setup is rigorous and covers a range of LLMs and tasks.
    *   The findings are clearly presented and supported by data.
    *   The analysis offers concrete practical suggestions for improving LLM inference.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The study is limited to tasks that can be easily represented as DFAs. While many common reasoning tasks fall into this category, the framework's applicability to more complex, unstructured tasks is unclear. It's crucial to note if there is any systematic bias to these solvable tasks, or if their generalizability would actually be useful in downstream tasks.
    *   The prediction model used for critical length is quite simple (linear regression). While it shows promising results, more sophisticated methods could potentially lead to even greater accuracy improvements.

*   **Potential Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence future research directions in LLM reasoning and inference. The DFA-based framework could be extended to analyze other aspects of task complexity and model behavior. The findings could also inform the design of more efficient and accurate LLM inference strategies.

**Justification:**

The paper's contribution is valuable. The rigor and potential for impact in optimizing LLM inference are evident. However, the limited scope of tasks to DFA-representable scenarios restricts broader applicability. Furthermore, while the results of critical length predictions are helpful, more sophisticated methods could exist that would have been good to mention and account for.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### A Unified Approach to Analysis and Design of Denoising Markov Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.01938v1)
- **Authors**: Yinuo Ren, Grant M. Rotskoff, Lexing Ying
- **Abstract**: Probabilistic generative models based on measure transport, such as diffusion and flow-based models, are often formulated in the language of Markovian stochastic dynamics, where the choice of the underlying process impacts both algorithmic design choices and theoretical analysis. In this paper, we aim to establish a rigorous mathematical foundation for denoising Markov models, a broad class of generative models that postulate a forward process transitioning from the target distribution to a simple, easy-to-sample distribution, alongside a backward process particularly constructed to enable efficient sampling in the reverse direction. Leveraging deep connections with nonequilibrium statistical mechanics and generalized Doob's $h$-transform, we propose a minimal set of assumptions that ensure: (1) explicit construction of the backward generator, (2) a unified variational objective directly minimizing the measure transport discrepancy, and (3) adaptations of the classical score-matching approach across diverse dynamics. Our framework unifies existing formulations of continuous and discrete diffusion models, identifies the most general form of denoising Markov models under certain regularity assumptions on forward generators, and provides a systematic recipe for designing denoising Markov models driven by arbitrary L\'evy-type processes. We illustrate the versatility and practical effectiveness of our approach through novel denoising Markov models employing geometric Brownian motion and jump processes as forward dynamics, highlighting the framework's potential flexibility and capability in modeling complex distributions.
- **Summary**: This paper presents a unified theoretical framework for analyzing and designing denoising Markov models (DMMs) for generative modeling. It leverages connections with nonequilibrium statistical mechanics and generalized Doob's h-transform to establish a mathematical foundation for this broad class of models. The authors propose a minimal set of assumptions ensuring (1) explicit construction of the backward generator, (2) a unified variational objective directly minimizing the measure transport discrepancy (KL divergence), and (3) adaptations of score-matching across diverse dynamics. The framework unifies continuous and discrete diffusion models, identifies the most general form of DMMs under regularity conditions, and provides a recipe for designing DMMs driven by arbitrary Lévy-type processes. The authors illustrate the approach with novel DMMs employing geometric Brownian motion and jump processes.

**Novelty and Significance Evaluation:**

The paper's strength lies in its ambitious goal of providing a unified and mathematically rigorous treatment of denoising Markov models. While the individual components (score matching, change of measure, Doob's h-transform) are not entirely new, the synthesis of these tools into a coherent framework for DMMs, specifically at the *generator level*, is a significant contribution. This generator-centric approach offers a level of abstraction that transcends specific process choices (e.g., diffusion, jump), allowing for a more general analysis and design methodology.

The paper explicitly addresses some limitations of previous works, like the lack of a general framework for both continuous and discrete diffusion models. By operating directly on generators, the work is able to unify continuous and discrete formulations under a single theoretical umbrella.

The authors demonstrate the flexibility of their framework by developing novel DMMs using geometric Brownian motion and jump processes. These examples, although relatively simple tasks, showcase the potential of the framework to explore less conventional forward processes beyond standard diffusion. While the empirical results aren't groundbreaking, they provide proof-of-concept for the theoretical developments. This is somewhat of a weakness; more complex experiments exhibiting significant improvement in generation quality would considerably strengthen the paper.

A potential weakness is the reliance on several regularity assumptions, particularly those pertaining to the densities and ratios of densities involved. While these assumptions are necessary for mathematical rigor, they might limit the practical applicability of the framework to certain types of distributions and processes. A more in-depth discussion on the practical implications and limitations of these assumptions would be valuable.

Furthermore, the connection to generator matching (Holderrieth et al., 2024) seems more of an acknowledgement than a deep comparison/extension.  A more thorough comparison of the pros/cons of each framework, as well as a rigorous explanation of its advantages would be crucial. The lack of direct comparison in the experiments is also a limitation.

Overall, the paper makes a valuable theoretical contribution to the field by providing a unifying perspective on denoising Markov models and offering a general framework for designing new generative models. The work has the potential to influence future research by encouraging exploration of diverse forward processes and providing a solid foundation for analyzing the properties of DMMs. However, more significant experimental demonstrations and a more extensive discussion on practical limitations would greatly improve the paper's impact.

Score: 7
The paper exhibits significant theoretical depth and contributes a novel unified framework. However, the experimental validation is somewhat limited, and a more thorough discussion of the assumptions and their practical implications is needed. This places the paper in the 'good' range.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### OpenCodeReasoning: Advancing Data Distillation for Competitive Coding
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.01943v1)
- **Authors**: Wasi Uddin Ahmad, Sean Narenthiran, Somshubra Majumdar, Aleksander Ficek, Siddhartha Jain, Jocelyn Huang, Vahid Noroozi, Boris Ginsburg
- **Abstract**: Since the advent of reasoning-based large language models, many have found great success from distilling reasoning capabilities into student models. Such techniques have significantly bridged the gap between reasoning and standard LLMs on coding tasks. Despite this, much of the progress on distilling reasoning models remains locked behind proprietary datasets or lacks details on data curation, filtering and subsequent training. To address this, we construct a superior supervised fine-tuning (SFT) dataset that we use to achieve state-of-the-art coding capability results in models of various sizes. Our distilled models use only SFT to achieve 61.8% on LiveCodeBench and 24.6% on CodeContests, surpassing alternatives trained with reinforcement learning. We then perform analysis on the data sources used to construct our dataset, the impact of code execution filtering, and the importance of instruction/solution diversity. We observe that execution filtering negatively affected benchmark accuracy, leading us to prioritize instruction diversity over solution correctness. Finally, we also analyze the token efficiency and reasoning patterns utilized by these models. We will open-source these datasets and distilled models to the community.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces OPENCODEREASONING, a large-scale dataset for supervised fine-tuning (SFT) of language models (LLMs) to improve their coding capabilities. The dataset consists of 736,712 code solutions (primarily Python) generated by a reasoning-based LLM (DeepSeek-R1) for 28,904 unique competitive programming questions.  The authors demonstrate that fine-tuning Qwen2.5 models (7B, 14B, and 32B parameters) with OPENCODEREASONING achieves state-of-the-art performance on LiveCodeBench and CodeContests benchmarks, surpassing SFT-only alternatives and approaching the performance of models trained with reinforcement learning (RL). The paper includes ablations on data filtering, language inclusion (C++), and analysis of reasoning trace lengths and patterns.  The authors highlight the importance of instruction diversity over strict solution correctness in dataset construction.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the *scale* of the released dataset, OPENCODEREASONING. While the approach of using LLMs to generate training data and then using SFT is not entirely new, the sheer size of this dataset surpasses existing public options. Furthermore, their comprehensive analysis is a significant contribution. The dataset creation recipe is well-documented and replicable.
*   **Significance:** The paper's significance is mixed.
    *   **Positive:** The demonstration that SFT alone, with a sufficiently large and well-curated dataset, can achieve competitive coding performance compared to RL-trained models is valuable. This lowers the barrier to entry for improving coding LLMs, as RL is often more complex to implement. Their analysis of various training strategies (data filtering, etc.) provide useful insight for future research.
    *   **Negative:** The paper relies on a proprietary, closed-source model (DeepSeek-R1) for data generation. This limits the reproducibility of the dataset creation process by the broader community. While they release the *dataset* itself, the generation process is a black box. It means that the community cannot improve the dataset. Also, the reliance on the Qwen model family is also limiting. A stronger paper would have evaluated a more comprehensive range of model families (e.g., Llama). Their analysis also found that instruction diversity mattered more than code correctness. While they analyzed the patterns in LLM reasoning, its overall impact is small as the results are not surprising.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Large-scale dataset: The sheer size of OPENCODEREASONING is a valuable contribution.
    *   Strong empirical results: The fine-tuned models achieve impressive performance on standard coding benchmarks.
    *   Thorough ablation study: The analysis of data filtering, reasoning patterns, and language inclusion provides valuable insights.
    *   Detailed documentation: The paper clearly describes the dataset construction process and experimental setup, promoting reproducibility.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Dependency on closed-source models for dataset generation: Limits reproducibility and potential for community-driven improvement of the *generation process*.
    *   Limited model family evaluation: Only evaluated Qwen models, which may not generalize to other LLM architectures.
    *   The key insights on filtering and correctness may be too specific to the DeepSeek-R1 model, not generalizable.

*   **Potential Influence:** The release of OPENCODEREASONING is likely to have a positive influence on the field by:
    *   Providing a readily available dataset for training and evaluating coding LLMs.
    *   Encouraging further research into SFT techniques for coding.
    *   Stimulating investigation into the importance of instruction diversity and data curation strategies.
    *   Providing a replicable recipe for future datasets detailing data sourcing and filtering methods.

Despite some weaknesses, the paper presents a substantial dataset and provides useful analysis that advances the understanding of how to improve coding capabilities of LLMs.

**Score: 7**

**Justification:** The paper's primary strength is the scale of the dataset and resulting empirical gains. However, the dependence on a closed-source LLM for generation limits its long-term impact. It still provides a valuable foundation for future work.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### The LLM Wears Prada: Analysing Gender Bias and Stereotypes through Online Shopping Data
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.01951v1)
- **Authors**: Massimiliano Luca, Ciro Beneduce, Bruno Lepri, Jacopo Staiano
- **Abstract**: With the wide and cross-domain adoption of Large Language Models, it becomes crucial to assess to which extent the statistical correlations in training data, which underlie their impressive performance, hide subtle and potentially troubling biases. Gender bias in LLMs has been widely investigated from the perspectives of works, hobbies, and emotions typically associated with a specific gender. In this study, we introduce a novel perspective. We investigate whether LLMs can predict an individual's gender based solely on online shopping histories and whether these predictions are influenced by gender biases and stereotypes. Using a dataset of historical online purchases from users in the United States, we evaluate the ability of six LLMs to classify gender and we then analyze their reasoning and products-gender co-occurrences. Results indicate that while models can infer gender with moderate accuracy, their decisions are often rooted in stereotypical associations between product categories and gender. Furthermore, explicit instructions to avoid bias reduce the certainty of model predictions, but do not eliminate stereotypical patterns. Our findings highlight the persistent nature of gender biases in LLMs and emphasize the need for robust bias-mitigation strategies.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "The LLM Wears Prada: Analysing Gender Bias and Stereotypes through Online Shopping Data":

**Summary:**

The paper explores gender bias and stereotypes in Large Language Models (LLMs) using a novel perspective: online shopping data. The authors examine whether LLMs can accurately predict a user's gender based solely on their purchase history and if these predictions are influenced by gender biases. Using a dataset of historical Amazon purchases, they evaluate the performance of six LLMs, analyzing their classification accuracy, reasoning processes, and product-gender co-occurrences. The study reveals that while LLMs can infer gender with some accuracy, they often rely on stereotypical associations between product categories and gender. Furthermore, explicit instructions to avoid bias only reduce the certainty of predictions but fail to eliminate underlying stereotypical patterns. The findings highlight the persistent nature of gender biases in LLMs and the need for more effective mitigation strategies.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in its **application of online shopping data** to investigate gender bias in LLMs. While gender bias in LLMs has been extensively studied, previous research typically focused on occupations, emotions, or language biases. Using purchase history provides a different lens, reflecting real-world consumer behavior. The study builds on existing research on LLM bias, but it doesn't introduce new methodologies for bias measurement or mitigation. It does contribute a new dataset and analysis strategy which is appreciated.
* **Significance:** The paper is significant because it demonstrates that LLMs can propagate and potentially amplify gender stereotypes even in a domain seemingly unrelated to traditional biases. The finding that LLMs not only reflect existing societal biases but can also exacerbate them has implications for applications like recommendation systems and targeted advertising. The observation that simple bias-mitigation prompts are insufficient further underscores the need for more robust approaches. The study has some relevance to the fairness community, the work is not particularly theoretical or technical. It would be more useful if the authors compared against more existing debiasing techniques.
* **Strengths:**
    *   **Novel data source:** The use of real-world purchasing data provides a more ecologically valid approach to studying gender bias than relying solely on text corpora.
    *   **Comprehensive analysis:** The paper combines quantitative (classification accuracy, JS divergence) and qualitative (analysis of model explanations) methods to provide a nuanced understanding of the issue.
    *   **Relevance:** The findings have practical implications for the development and deployment of LLMs in commercial applications.

* **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Binary gender assumption:** The study's focus on binary gender classification (male/female) is a significant limitation, as it ignores the diversity of gender identities. It is necessary to acknowledge that gender is a spectrum.
    *   **Limited scope:** The reliance on a single dataset from Amazon purchases restricts the generalizability of the findings. Different e-commerce platforms or geographic regions might yield different results.
    *   **Lack of a theoretical grounding:** The paper could benefit from a stronger theoretical framework for understanding the relationship between LLM training data, model architecture, and the emergence of gender biases.
    *   **No bias mitigation methods are compared against:** While the paper shows simple prompting doesn't help, more modern debiasing method should be considered.
* **Potential Influence:** The paper's findings could encourage further research into the use of alternative data sources for studying LLM bias and the development of more effective bias-mitigation techniques. The study could also raise awareness among developers and policymakers about the potential for LLMs to reinforce harmful gender stereotypes.

Overall, the paper makes a valuable contribution by applying a novel perspective to a well-established problem. While the study has some limitations, the findings are important and could stimulate further research in this area.

**Score: 6.5**

**Rationale:**

The novelty and significance are adequate, but not groundbreaking. The core idea of using shopping data to uncover bias is appealing, and the results are relevant to real-world applications. However, the methodological contributions are limited, and the reliance on a binary gender construct restricts the scope and impact of the study. The lack of bias mitigation comparison is also a concern. I have increased the score for the care the authors took in the experiment and analysis phases.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 6/10

### VideoScene: Distilling Video Diffusion Model to Generate 3D Scenes in One Step
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.01956v2)
- **Authors**: Hanyang Wang, Fangfu Liu, Jiawei Chi, Yueqi Duan
- **Abstract**: Recovering 3D scenes from sparse views is a challenging task due to its inherent ill-posed problem. Conventional methods have developed specialized solutions (e.g., geometry regularization or feed-forward deterministic model) to mitigate the issue. However, they still suffer from performance degradation by minimal overlap across input views with insufficient visual information. Fortunately, recent video generative models show promise in addressing this challenge as they are capable of generating video clips with plausible 3D structures. Powered by large pretrained video diffusion models, some pioneering research start to explore the potential of video generative prior and create 3D scenes from sparse views. Despite impressive improvements, they are limited by slow inference time and the lack of 3D constraint, leading to inefficiencies and reconstruction artifacts that do not align with real-world geometry structure. In this paper, we propose VideoScene to distill the video diffusion model to generate 3D scenes in one step, aiming to build an efficient and effective tool to bridge the gap from video to 3D. Specifically, we design a 3D-aware leap flow distillation strategy to leap over time-consuming redundant information and train a dynamic denoising policy network to adaptively determine the optimal leap timestep during inference. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our VideoScene achieves faster and superior 3D scene generation results than previous video diffusion models, highlighting its potential as an efficient tool for future video to 3D applications. Project Page: https://hanyang-21.github.io/VideoScene
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the VideoScene paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces VideoScene, a novel framework designed to generate 3D scenes efficiently from sparse views (specifically, two input images).  It addresses the limitations of existing video diffusion models, which, while capable of plausible 3D structures, suffer from slow inference times and lack explicit 3D constraints. VideoScene distills video diffusion models into a one-step process by employing a "3D-aware leap flow distillation" strategy. This involves using a fast, feed-forward 3DGS model to create a coarse but 3D-consistent initial scene, followed by a dynamic denoising policy network (DDPNet) to adaptively select optimal leap timesteps during inference, balancing noise addition with information retention. The authors demonstrate that VideoScene achieves faster and superior 3D scene generation compared to previous video diffusion methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of distilling video diffusion models for efficient 3D scene generation is reasonably novel. The 3D-aware leap flow distillation and the DDPNet are specific contributions designed to improve the efficiency and 3D consistency. While distillation has been applied to other generative models, the application to video diffusion for *sparse view* 3D scene generation, along with the specific techniques to maintain 3D awareness, is a distinctive contribution. However, distillation is a well-established technique, reducing the *relative* novelty of the entire approach.
*   **Significance:** The potential impact lies in bridging the gap between powerful video generation models and practical 3D reconstruction applications. The inefficiency of directly applying video diffusion models to 3D tasks is a clear bottleneck, and VideoScene directly addresses this. Faster inference times and improved 3D consistency are crucial for enabling real-time or interactive applications. The results demonstrate a significant improvement in speed and quality over existing approaches, which could accelerate research and development in areas like virtual reality, augmented reality, and robotics. However, many of the experiments rely on fairly synthetic data. The reliance on explicit camera pose information may also limit the application to truly "in the wild" scenarios where pose estimation is noisy or unavailable.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem statement and well-defined goal.
    *   Technically sound approach with reasonable justification for design choices.
    *   Comprehensive experimental evaluation demonstrating improved speed and quality.
    *   Addresses a practical bottleneck in video diffusion for 3D scene generation.
    * Well written and easy to understand.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Relatively high reliance on a good initialization from a feed forward network, in order to make denoising work. In instances of a bad initialization, the video gen can yield less plausible results.
    * While the approach distills the *attention* layers, it still requires a massive pre-trained video generation back bone. This reliance is a practical limitation.
    * The dependency on accurate camera pose restricts the use to scenarios where this is reliably available.
    * The paper might benefit from more detailed ablation studies to dissect the individual contributions of the 3D prior and DDPNet more fully.
*   **Potential Influence:** If the approach proves to be robust and generalizable beyond the specific datasets used in the evaluation, it could significantly influence the field by:
    *   Encouraging further research into distillation techniques for generative models in 3D applications.
    *   Motivating the development of more efficient and controllable video generation models.
    *   Enabling new applications that require real-time or interactive 3D scene reconstruction.

Overall, the paper makes a solid contribution to the field of 3D scene generation from sparse views. The distillation approach, combined with the 3D-aware techniques, offers a compelling balance between speed and quality. However, the limitations related to reliance on existing video models and camera pose information should not be ignored.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### From Prompts to Templates: A Systematic Prompt Template Analysis for Real-world LLMapps
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.02052v1)
- **Authors**: Yuetian Mao, Junjie He, Chunyang Chen
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have revolutionized human-AI interaction by enabling intuitive task execution through natural language prompts. Despite their potential, designing effective prompts remains a significant challenge, as small variations in structure or wording can result in substantial differences in output. To address these challenges, LLM-powered applications (LLMapps) rely on prompt templates to simplify interactions, enhance usability, and support specialized tasks such as document analysis, creative content generation, and code synthesis. However, current practices heavily depend on individual expertise and iterative trial-and-error processes, underscoring the need for systematic methods to optimize prompt template design in LLMapps. This paper presents a comprehensive analysis of prompt templates in practical LLMapps. We construct a dataset of real-world templates from open-source LLMapps, including those from leading companies like Uber and Microsoft. Through a combination of LLM-driven analysis and human review, we categorize template components and placeholders, analyze their distributions, and identify frequent co-occurrence patterns. Additionally, we evaluate the impact of identified patterns on LLMs' instruction-following performance through sample testing. Our findings provide practical insights on prompt template design for developers, supporting the broader adoption and optimization of LLMapps in industrial settings.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "From Prompts to Templates: A Systematic Prompt Template Analysis for Real-world LLMapps" addresses the challenges of designing effective prompt templates for Large Language Model-powered applications (LLMapps). It conducts a comprehensive analysis of prompt templates collected from open-source LLMapps, including those from major companies like Uber and Microsoft. The authors categorize template components and placeholders, analyze their distributions, identify co-occurrence patterns, and evaluate the impact of these patterns on LLMs' instruction-following performance.  The goal is to provide practical insights for developers to improve prompt template design, standardize user interactions, and enhance the performance of LLMapps. Key findings include identifying common prompt components, the order in which they are placed, JSON output format patterns, constraints to refine LLM outputs, and the positioning of knowledge input placeholders.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Empirical Approach:** The paper leverages a large dataset of real-world prompt templates, providing a strong empirical basis for its analysis. This distinguishes it from more theoretical or small-scale studies. The selection process for data and the data cleaning process is also described in detail.

    *   **Comprehensive Analysis:**  The analysis is thorough, covering different facets of prompt templates: components, placeholders, positional order, JSON output styles, and constraint types. The categorization is well-defined.

    *   **Practical Relevance:** The findings offer actionable insights for LLMapp developers. The identification of common components, patterns, and constraints can directly inform the design of more effective prompt templates. The analysis on the influence of design choice on content following and formatting is also very helpful.

    *   **Reproducibility:** The dataset is made publicly available which is an excellent contribution, fostering reproducibility and further research.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Generalizability & Limited LLM Focus:**  The evaluation, while detailed, primarily focuses on only two LLMs: Llama3-70b-8192 and gpt-4o. While these are significant, generalizability of findings to different model architectures and model sizes should be acknowledged as a limitation. It is possible that the effectiveness of the extracted patterns may vary for different LLM families.

    *   **Reliance on LLMs for Analysis:** The heavy reliance on LLMs for component identification (e.g., using Llama3-70b-8192 to identify components) raises concerns about circularity. If the LLM's understanding of components is flawed, it could bias the analysis. While human evaluation is included, the potential bias should be more explicitly addressed.

    *   **Potential for Bias in Placeholder Categories:**  The iterative process for defining placeholder categories, while involving human review, may still be influenced by initial manual classifications, which could introduce bias.

    *   **Limited Scope of Evaluation:** While the paper examines *some* downstream effects through sample testing (e.g., JSON output), it would be stronger if it included experiments on full LLMapp scenarios. The analysis might be even more significant if it assessed the actual impact of the optimized prompt template design on downstream tasks in a broader context.

    *   **Limited Discussion of In-Context Learning:** The limited scope of in-context learning in prompt templates could be discussed in more detail.
*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    *   The paper is novel in its systematic analysis of real-world prompt templates used in LLMapps. Most existing work focuses on general prompt engineering or smaller, curated datasets.

    *   The work is highly significant because it helps to bridge the gap between theoretical prompt engineering guidelines and the practical needs of LLMapp developers. By identifying common patterns and assessing their impact, the paper contributes to a more evidence-based approach to prompt template design.

    *   The study provides some clear and concrete directions on how to design a prompt template, while previous research often focused on general principles.
**Overall Score and Justification:**

I assign a score of **7.5**. The paper presents a valuable and well-executed empirical study of prompt templates in LLMapps. The analysis is comprehensive, the findings are practical, and the dataset is publicly available. However, the limited generalizability due to the focus on few LLMs, potential biases from LLM-assisted analysis, and narrow scope of evaluation prevent it from reaching a higher score. Nevertheless, the work makes a significant contribution to the field by providing much-needed empirical evidence and actionable insights for improving prompt template design in LLMapps.
Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 7/10

### MageSQL: Enhancing In-context Learning for Text-to-SQL Applications with Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.02055v1)
- **Authors**: Chen Shen, Jin Wang, Sajjadur Rahman, Eser Kandogan
- **Abstract**: The text-to-SQL problem aims to translate natural language questions into SQL statements to ease the interaction between database systems and end users. Recently, Large Language Models (LLMs) have exhibited impressive capabilities in a variety of tasks, including text-to-SQL. While prior works have explored various strategies for prompting LLMs to generate SQL statements, they still fall short of fully harnessing the power of LLM due to the lack of (1) high-quality contextual information when constructing the prompts and (2) robust feedback mechanisms to correct translation errors. To address these challenges, we propose MageSQL, a text-to-SQL approach based on in-context learning over LLMs. MageSQL explores a suite of techniques that leverage the syntax and semantics of SQL queries to identify relevant few-shot demonstrations as context for prompting LLMs. In particular, we introduce a graph-based demonstration selection method -- the first of its kind in the text-to-SQL problem -- that leverages graph contrastive learning adapted with SQL-specific data augmentation strategies. Furthermore, an error correction module is proposed to detect and fix potential inaccuracies in the generated SQL query. We conduct comprehensive evaluations on several benchmarking datasets. The results show that our proposed methods outperform state-of-the-art methods by an obvious margin.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "MageSQL: Enhancing In-context Learning for Text-to-SQL Applications with Large Language Models" proposes a new framework to improve the performance of Large Language Models (LLMs) in the text-to-SQL task. MageSQL focuses on two main aspects: (1) selecting high-quality demonstration examples for in-context learning, and (2) implementing an error correction module to address potential inaccuracies in the generated SQL. The authors introduce a graph-based demonstration selection method that leverages graph contrastive learning, augmented with SQL-specific data augmentation strategies, and combine this with structure-based selection. They also develop rule-based and prompt-based error correction methods.  Experimental results on Spider and BIRD datasets demonstrate improvements over state-of-the-art methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Addresses a Key Limitation:** The paper directly tackles the critical problem of prompt engineering for LLMs in the text-to-SQL context, specifically focusing on demonstration selection and error correction, which are known bottlenecks.
*   **Novel Demonstration Selection:** The introduction of a graph-based demonstration selection method that captures both structural and semantic information of SQL queries is a significant contribution. The use of graph contrastive learning for this purpose is novel and well-justified. Combining it with structure-based selection further strengthens the approach.
*   **Comprehensive Error Correction:** The error correction module, combining rule-based and prompt-based techniques, is a practical addition. It acknowledges the inherent uncertainties in LLM outputs and provides a mechanism for improvement.
*   **Empirical Validation:** The paper presents thorough experimental evaluations on standard benchmark datasets (Spider and BIRD) and demonstrates substantial performance gains over existing methods.
*   **Detailed Analysis:** The paper includes a detailed error analysis, which provides insights into the types of errors that still occur and guides future research directions.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Complexity and Overhead:** The graph-based demonstration selection method introduces computational overhead, particularly in constructing and embedding the SQL graphs, this is confirmed by the Token analysis in the paper. While the pq-gram estimation helps reduce the cost, the overall complexity of this approach might be a concern for real-time applications.
*   **Dependence on LLM Capabilities:** The prompt-based error correction relies on the ability of LLMs to understand and correct SQL queries, which is not always guaranteed. If the initial SQL generation is fundamentally flawed, the error correction might struggle to produce a valid output. The paper should discuss the limitations of this approach.
*   **Limited Error Correction Scope:** While the error correction module addresses some common errors, it might not be able to handle all types of inaccuracies. Further research is needed to develop more robust error correction techniques. Also, this only performs error analysis on the Spider dataset which could result in limited insights on other datasets.
*   **Limited Comparison Baselines**: In Table III, the results for a break-down analysis is only limited to two baselines of CatSQL and DIN-SQL. This makes it hard to do any meaningful comparison and analysis on a per-category basis.

**Novelty and Significance:**

The paper makes several significant contributions:

*   It introduces a novel graph-based approach to demonstration selection that leverages graph contrastive learning.
*   It proposes a combined rule-based and prompt-based error correction module to improve the accuracy of generated SQL.
*   It provides a comprehensive evaluation of the proposed methods on standard benchmark datasets, demonstrating significant performance gains.

The paper has the potential to influence future research in the field of text-to-SQL by providing a more effective approach to prompt engineering for LLMs. The graph-based demonstration selection method and the error correction module can be adapted and extended to other tasks as well.

**Score: 8**

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a well-motivated and technically sound approach to improve the performance of LLMs in the text-to-SQL task. The introduction of a graph-based demonstration selection method is novel and significant. The error correction module further enhances the practical applicability of the proposed framework. The empirical results are convincing and demonstrate substantial improvements over existing methods. The paper acknowledges the limitations of the approach and provides insights for future research. The score of 8 reflects the paper's strong contributions to the field, while also recognizing some limitations in computational complexity, dependence on LLM capabilities, and error correction scope.

- **Classification**: cs.DB
- **Score**: 8/10

### Towards Operationalizing Heterogeneous Data Discovery
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.02059v1)
- **Authors**: Jin Wang, Yanlin Feng, Chen Shen, Sajjadur Rahman, Eser Kandogan
- **Abstract**: Querying and exploring massive collections of data sources, such as data lakes, has been an essential research topic in the database community. Although many efforts have been paid in the field of data discovery and data integration in data lakes, they mainly focused on the scenario where the data lake consists of structured tables. However, real-world enterprise data lakes are always more complicated, where there might be silos of multi-modal data sources with structured, semi-structured and unstructured data. In this paper, we envision an end-to-end system with declarative interface for querying and analyzing the multi-modal data lakes. First of all, we come up with a set of multi-modal operators, which is a unified interface that extends the relational operations with AI-composed ones to express analytical workloads over data sources in various modalities. In addition, we formally define the essential steps in the system, such as data discovery, query planning, query processing and results aggregation. On the basis of it, we then pinpoint the research challenges and discuss potential opportunities in realizing and optimizing them with advanced techniques brought by Large Language Models. Finally, we demonstrate our preliminary attempts to address this problem and suggest the future plan for this research topic.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and critical evaluation of the paper, "Towards Operationalizing Heterogeneous Data Discovery."

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of querying and analyzing data in modern enterprise data lakes, which are often characterized by heterogeneous data sources with multi-modal data (structured tables, semi-structured graphs, unstructured text). The authors envision an end-to-end system with a declarative query interface capable of handling such complexity.  The proposed system includes: (1) a set of multi-modal operators extending relational operations with AI-composed ones, (2) data discovery mechanisms to identify relevant data sources, (3) query planning and optimization components, and (4) a result aggregation process to combine outputs from different sources. The paper outlines the system architecture, discusses research challenges and opportunities, particularly concerning the application of Large Language Models (LLMs), and presents preliminary benchmarks for evaluating data discovery components. It introduces CMDBench for multimodal discovery and CypherBench for graph query generation from natural language.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper tackles a relevant and important problem.  The increasing prevalence of heterogeneous data lakes necessitates tools and systems capable of seamlessly querying and analyzing data across different modalities. The *vision* of a unified, declarative query interface is appealing and addresses a significant pain point in data management.

However, several aspects limit the paper's novelty and significance:

*   **Incremental Contribution:** The components presented, while logically sound, largely build upon existing work. Multi-modal data processing, data discovery, and query optimization are all well-established areas.  The novelty lies primarily in *combining* these elements into a single envisioned system, but the individual components do not appear to offer breakthrough advancements.
*   **Lack of Concretization:**  The paper remains at a relatively high level of abstraction. While it describes the system's modules and the multi-modal operators, concrete implementation details and specific algorithms are scarce. The discussion of query planning and optimization, in particular, lacks depth and relies heavily on general concepts.
*   **Limited Evaluation:** The evaluation is primarily focused on data discovery, which is just one aspect of the envisioned system.  While the introduction of CMDBench and CypherBench is a positive contribution, these are still relatively preliminary benchmarks. The paper provides minimal evaluation of end-to-end performance or the effectiveness of the proposed query planning or result aggregation techniques. The results presented related to the benchmark only focus on discovery models, it is unclear whether the multi-modal operators and overall query plan will benefit from the enhanced discovery outcome.
*   **Over-reliance on LLMs (Potential):** The paper discusses using LLMs for various tasks, such as data discovery, operator predicates, and result aggregation. While LLMs offer interesting possibilities, the paper doesn't critically assess the potential drawbacks (e.g., cost, latency, reliability, lack of interpretability) or propose specific techniques to mitigate them. Without careful consideration, LLM integration could easily become a performance bottleneck or introduce inaccuracies. The paper does not have any effort to benchmark the LLMs on multi-modal data lakes.
*   **Missing Related Work:** While the related work is mentioned, some key areas could be expanded. For example,  more discussion regarding prior art in data fusion and semantic integration would strengthen the context.

**Strengths:**

*   Addresses an important and timely problem.
*   Presents a comprehensive vision of a multi-modal data lake querying system.
*   Identifies key research challenges and opportunities.
*   Introduces CMDBench and CypherBench as initial benchmarks for data discovery and graph query generation.

**Weaknesses:**

*   Lacks deep technical contributions in individual components.
*   Limited concrete implementation details and specific algorithms.
*   Insufficient end-to-end evaluation of the envisioned system.
*   Potentially over-reliant on LLMs without critical evaluation.
*   Lacks detailed description of query optimization and planning algorithms.

**Justification:**

The paper's vision is valuable, but its technical contributions are incremental. The lack of concrete implementation details and comprehensive evaluation limits its immediate impact. While the benchmarks are a positive step, more extensive experimentation is needed to demonstrate the system's effectiveness and practicality. The dependence of LLMs is promising but needs rigorous consideration of trade-offs and potential problems. Therefore, the paper is not at the level to have a very significant contribution to the field, but it is well deserving of some attention.

Score: 5

- **Classification**: cs.DB
- **Score**: 5/10

### Aligned Better, Listen Better for Audio-Visual Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.02061v1)
- **Authors**: Yuxin Guo, Shuailei Ma, Shijie Ma, Xiaoyi Bao, Chen-Wei Xie, Kecheng Zheng, Tingyu Weng, Siyang Sun, Yun Zheng, Wei Zou
- **Abstract**: Audio is essential for multimodal video understanding. On the one hand, video inherently contains audio, which supplies complementary information to vision. Besides, video large language models (Video-LLMs) can encounter many audio-centric settings. However, existing Video-LLMs and Audio-Visual Large Language Models (AV-LLMs) exhibit deficiencies in exploiting audio information, leading to weak understanding and hallucinations. To solve the issues, we delve into the model architecture and dataset. (1) From the architectural perspective, we propose a fine-grained AV-LLM, namely Dolphin. The concurrent alignment of audio and visual modalities in both temporal and spatial dimensions ensures a comprehensive and accurate understanding of videos. Specifically, we devise an audio-visual multi-scale adapter for multi-scale information aggregation, which achieves spatial alignment. For temporal alignment, we propose audio-visual interleaved merging. (2) From the dataset perspective, we curate an audio-visual caption and instruction-tuning dataset, called AVU. It comprises 5.2 million diverse, open-ended data tuples (video, audio, question, answer) and introduces a novel data partitioning strategy. Extensive experiments show our model not only achieves remarkable performance in audio-visual understanding, but also mitigates potential hallucinations.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Aligned Better, Listen Better for Audio-Visual Large Language Models," along with a justified score:

**Summary**

The paper addresses the limitations of existing Video-LLMs and AV-LLMs in effectively utilizing audio information for video understanding. The authors argue that these models often neglect audio, leading to weak understanding and hallucinations. To address this, they propose two key contributions:

1.  **Dolphin:** A fine-grained AV-LLM architecture that achieves concurrent alignment of audio and visual modalities in both temporal and spatial dimensions. This involves an audio-visual multi-scale adapter for spatial alignment and audio-visual interleaved merging for temporal alignment.

2.  **AVU Dataset:** A large-scale audio-visual captioning and instruction-tuning dataset comprising 5.2 million video, audio, question, and answer tuples. The dataset is curated with a novel data partitioning strategy and incorporates various AV tasks.

The authors demonstrate through experiments that their model achieves superior performance in audio-visual understanding and mitigates hallucinations.

**Critical Evaluation**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Problem Significance:** The paper tackles a relevant and important problem: the underutilization of audio in video understanding by LLMs. This is a clear limitation in current multimodal models.
    *   **Architectural Novelty:** The proposed Dolphin architecture presents a novel approach to audio-visual alignment. The multi-scale adapter and interleaved merging techniques seem well-designed to capture both spatial and temporal relationships between audio and video.
    *   **Dataset Contribution:** The creation of the AVU dataset is a significant contribution. The size and diversity of the dataset, along with its novel partitioning strategy, could provide a valuable resource for the community. The dataset fills a recognized need for improved audio-visual training data.
    *   **Experimental Validation:** The experiments are comprehensive and demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed approach. The ablation studies provide insights into the importance of each component of the Dolphin architecture and the AVU dataset. Comparisons against strong baselines (Video-LLaMA) highlight the gains achieved.
    *   **Mitigation of Hallucinations:** A key selling point is the model's ability to mitigate hallucinations, addressing a critical concern in LLMs and further addressed by the addition of negative examples in the data.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Incremental Novelty:** While the architecture is novel, the components (e.g., cross-attention, multi-scale adapters) are not entirely new. The novelty lies in the specific combination and application to the audio-visual alignment problem.
    *   **Complexity:** The architecture is relatively complex, which could make it challenging to implement and train.
    *   **Lack of Detail in Pre-processing:** Some description should be included about the pre-processing steps involved in cleaning and preparing the data. How was the quality of audio in these samples tested, and how did you approach integrating samples of varying quality?
    *   **Compute Cost:** The paper provides few details on compute requirements; training this model is likely resource-intensive, which may limit its accessibility to some researchers.
    *   **Generalizability:** Although the paper touches upon the generalizability of its model, more could be done to show that Dolphin outperforms competing methodologies when it comes to generating responses for video understanding tasks from a diverse set of sources.

*   **Significance:**
    *   The paper has the potential to significantly influence the development of more capable and reliable audio-visual LLMs.
    *   The AVU dataset could become a standard benchmark for evaluating audio-visual understanding.
    *   The techniques developed in this paper could be applied to other multimodal learning tasks.

*   **Justification of Score:**

This paper presents a solid contribution to the field of multimodal learning, specifically addressing the often-overlooked role of audio in video understanding for large language models. The strengths of the work lie in its architectural innovations – the audio-visual multi-scale adapter and audio-visual interleaved merging – and the creation of a substantial, thoughtfully curated dataset. The experimental results are convincing, clearly demonstrating the improvements achieved by Dolphin, especially concerning hallucination mitigation.

However, the novelty, while present, is incremental, building upon existing architectural components. The complexity of the model and the potentially high computational cost of training could limit its adoption.

Weighing these factors, a score of 7 seems appropriate. It reflects a valuable and well-executed contribution with potential for substantial impact, but acknowledges limitations in fundamental architectural innovation and potentially high resource demands.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### From Text to Graph: Leveraging Graph Neural Networks for Enhanced Explainability in NLP
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.02064v1)
- **Authors**: Fabio Yáñez-Romero, Andrés Montoyo, Armando Suárez, Yoan Gutiérrez, Ruslan Mitkov
- **Abstract**: Researchers have relegated natural language processing tasks to Transformer-type models, particularly generative models, because these models exhibit high versatility when performing generation and classification tasks. As the size of these models increases, they achieve outstanding results. Given their widespread use, many explainability techniques are developed based on these models. However, this process becomes computationally expensive due to the large size of the models. Additionally, transformers interpret input information through tokens that fragment input words into sequences lacking inherent semantic meaning, complicating the explanation of the model from the very beginning. This study proposes a novel methodology to achieve explainability in natural language processing tasks by automatically converting sentences into graphs and maintaining semantics through nodes and relations that express fundamental linguistic concepts. It also allows the subsequent exploitation of this knowledge in subsequent tasks, making it possible to obtain trends and understand how the model associates the different elements inside the text with the explained task. The experiments delivered promising results in determining the most critical components within the text structure for a given classification.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a novel methodology for explainable NLP by converting sentences into graph structures that maintain semantic meaning (words and relationships), as opposed to token-based approaches common in Transformer models.
