- **Score**: 8/10

### LLMPi: Optimizing LLMs for High-Throughput on Raspberry Pi
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.02118v1)
- **Authors**: Mahsa Ardakani, Jinendra Malekar, Ramtin Zand
- **Abstract**: Deploying Large Language Models (LLMs) on resource-constrained edge devices like the Raspberry Pi presents challenges in computational efficiency, power consumption, and response latency. This paper explores quantization-based optimization techniques to enable high-throughput, energy-efficient execution of LLMs on low-power embedded systems. Our approach leverages k-quantization, a Post-Training Quantization (PTQ) method designed for different bit-widths, enabling efficient 2-bit, 4-bit, 6-bit, and 8-bit weight quantization. Additionally, we employ ternary quantization using Quantization-Aware Training (QAT) for BitNet models, allowing for more effective adaptation to lower-bit representations while preserving accuracy. Our findings highlight the potential of quantized LLMs for real-time conversational AI on edge devices, paving the way for low-power, high-efficiency AI deployment in mobile and embedded applications. This study demonstrates that aggressive quantization strategies can significantly reduce energy consumption while maintaining inference quality, making LLMs practical for resource-limited environments.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a concise summary of the paper, followed by a rigorous and critical evaluation of its novelty and significance, and a justified score.

**Summary**

The paper "LLMPi: Optimizing LLMs for High-Throughput on Raspberry Pi" investigates quantization techniques to enable efficient deployment of large language models (LLMs) on resource-constrained edge devices like the Raspberry Pi. The authors explored post-training quantization (PTQ) methods, specifically k-quantization, to support different bit-widths (2-bit, 4-bit, 6-bit, and 8-bit). They also examine ternary quantization using quantization-aware training (QAT) for BitNet models. The results demonstrate the potential of quantized LLMs for real-time conversational AI on edge devices, focusing on reducing energy consumption while maintaining inference quality. They assess models in terms of throughput, energy efficiency, and response quality against FP16 baselines.

**Rigorous and Critical Evaluation**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in its empirical evaluation of various quantization techniques, including different bit widths and BitNet models, specifically targeting the Raspberry Pi platform. While quantization techniques themselves are not entirely new, the comprehensive examination of their performance across several LLMs and particularly focusing on a very constrained device is the core contribution. The use of k-quantization and the specific implementation details contribute incremental novelty. The application of ternary-weight models to BitNet and evaluating its performance against conventionally quantized models is a valuable comparison. The definition and measurement of metrics like 'Words per Battery Life' also offers incremental value in practically assessing the impact of deployed models.

*   **Significance:** The significance of this work resides in its practical implications for deploying LLMs in real-world, resource-limited environments. Edge deployment of LLMs offers advantages like reduced latency, improved privacy, and offline functionality, making this work relevant to applications like social robotics, mobile assistants, and embedded AI.  The work directly addresses the critical challenge of running computationally intensive LLMs on low-power devices, which is crucial for widespread adoption of AI-driven applications. Quantifying the trade-offs between accuracy, latency, and energy efficiency is important for choosing the appropriate quantization strategy for different use cases. Highlighting the effectiveness of BitNet's specialized bitwise operations further contributes to the field.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper provides a detailed and relatively comprehensive evaluation of different quantization techniques across multiple LLMs, making it a useful resource for practitioners.
    *   **Practical Focus:** The emphasis on energy efficiency and real-time performance metrics makes the study practically relevant.
    *   **Edge-Specific Considerations:**  The specific focus on Raspberry Pi addresses the constraints of low-power edge devices, a critical area for LLM deployment.
    *   **Comparative Analysis:** Comparing PTQ and QAT techniques (BitNet vs. quantized transformers) is insightful.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Novelty in Techniques:** The quantization methods themselves are not groundbreaking. K-Quantization and absmax are well-established, reducing innovation. The main work is the experimentation.
    *   **Hardware Specific:** The study's findings may be specific to the Raspberry Pi 5, limiting their generalizability to other edge devices. More rigorous hardware diversity testing would be beneficial.
    *   **Accuracy Metrics:** While the use of NUBIA is good, relying solely on it for semantic coherence isn't comprehensive. Additional metrics like perplexity or human evaluations would strengthen the analysis of quality degradation.
    *   **Context Length:** The prompt context length is limited to 4096, where modern LLMs are able to incorporate much longer context.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper could influence the development of more efficient LLM quantization techniques and the design of specialized hardware for edge AI. It provides valuable insights for researchers and engineers working on deploying LLMs in resource-constrained environments. It could also lead to broader adoption of AI-powered applications in domains like robotics and mobile computing, where energy efficiency is paramount.

*   **Justification for Score:**

While the paper performs a detailed evaluation, the techniques employed, such as quantization and absmax, are not novel in themselves. However, the combination of the specific quantizaiton techniques, combined with focus on low-resource devices and the comprehensive assessment of model accuracy, energy usage, and latency make this paper a meaningful contribution. It serves as a useful guide for choosing the right quantization strategies for Raspberry Pi deployments, and also shines a light on promising methods for energy optimization. The limited assessment of accuracy and reliance on a single hardware device, however, holds the score from going higher.

**Score: 6.5**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 6/10

### Efficient Model Selection for Time Series Forecasting via LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.02119v1)
- **Authors**: Wang Wei, Tiankai Yang, Hongjie Chen, Ryan A. Rossi, Yue Zhao, Franck Dernoncourt, Hoda Eldardiry
- **Abstract**: Model selection is a critical step in time series forecasting, traditionally requiring extensive performance evaluations across various datasets. Meta-learning approaches aim to automate this process, but they typically depend on pre-constructed performance matrices, which are costly to build. In this work, we propose to leverage Large Language Models (LLMs) as a lightweight alternative for model selection. Our method eliminates the need for explicit performance matrices by utilizing the inherent knowledge and reasoning capabilities of LLMs. Through extensive experiments with LLaMA, GPT and Gemini, we demonstrate that our approach outperforms traditional meta-learning techniques and heuristic baselines, while significantly reducing computational overhead. These findings underscore the potential of LLMs in efficient model selection for time series forecasting.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a novel approach to time series forecasting model selection using Large Language Models (LLMs).  Instead of relying on traditional meta-learning techniques that require extensive, pre-computed performance matrices, the authors leverage the inherent knowledge and reasoning abilities of LLMs via zero-shot prompting to select the most appropriate model for a given dataset. The method involves constructing prompts from dataset values (with optional inclusion of meta-features and chain-of-thought reasoning), querying an LLM to identify a suitable forecasting model and its hyperparameters, and then applying that model to generate forecasts and evaluate performance.  Through experiments on a diverse set of time series datasets, the authors demonstrate that their LLM-based selection approach outperforms traditional meta-learning methods and heuristic baselines, while also significantly reducing computational overhead.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The central idea of using LLMs for *model selection* in time series forecasting, rather than directly *as* forecasting models, is a valuable novelty.  It shifts the paradigm from training complex models to intelligently selecting existing ones. The paper is the first to investigate LLMs for this specific task. Furthermore, the elimination of the need for pre-computed performance matrices offers a significant advantage over meta-learning techniques.

* **Significance:** The work tackles a crucial problem in time series analysis: efficient model selection.  The approach offers a more computationally efficient and scalable alternative to traditional methods, potentially democratizing access to high-quality forecasting for users with limited resources. The substantial reduction in inference time demonstrated in the experiments (up to 89x compared to the naïve approach) underscores its practical significance.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the challenge of model selection in time series forecasting.
    * **Well-Defined Methodology:** The prompt construction and experimental setup are well-defined, allowing for reproducibility.
    * **Strong Empirical Results:** The experiments are comprehensive, comparing the LLM-based approach against diverse baselines and meta-learning methods across a substantial number of datasets. The paper demonstrates clear improvements in hit@k accuracy and MSE.
    * **Ablation Study:**  The ablation study on prompt design (meta-features, CoT reasoning) provides valuable insights into the effectiveness of different prompt components.
    * **Runtime Analysis:** The analysis of runtime performance clearly demonstrates the computational advantages of the LLM-based approach.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited Data Types:**  The study focuses exclusively on univariate time series data. Expanding to multivariate data would strengthen the generalizability.
    * **Dependence on Prompt Engineering:** While the paper explores prompt design, the performance of LLM-based methods is heavily dependent on effective prompt engineering, which can be challenging and time-consuming.  More guidance on best practices for prompt design would be beneficial.
    * **Lack of Explanation of Mechanisms:**  The paper offers some speculation, but ultimately the core reason why LLMs make *good selections* is not well-explained. More analysis of the internal reasoning of the LLMs would be valuable, even if through qualitative examples.
    * **Ethical Considerations:** The Ethics Statement is brief and generic.  A more thorough discussion of potential biases in the LLMs and the impact on forecasting outcomes would be useful.

* **Potential Influence:**  This work has the potential to significantly influence the field of time series forecasting by providing a more accessible and computationally efficient approach to model selection. It could also inspire further research into the use of LLMs for other tasks in time series analysis and related domains. It presents a compelling case for the power of LLMs to infer complex model choices without explicit training.

* **Rigor:** The paper's claims are well supported by experimental evidence. The statistical analysis is sound, and the methodology is clearly described. The comparisons to existing methods are comprehensive.

**Justification for Score:**

I am assigning a score of 7. The paper demonstrates a clear and impactful approach with compelling results, but it needs further refinement with more focus on data types to make it truly transformative. The lack of clear explanation for why LLMs excel at the task (other than attributing it to inherent knowledge) and the brief ethics statement further bring down the score. Despite these limitations, the work is of high quality and presents a novel direction for time series model selection with clear potential to impact the field.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Achieving Unanimous Consensus in Decision Making Using Multi-Agents
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.02128v1)
- **Authors**: Apurba Pokharel, Ram Dantu, Shakila Zaman, Sirisha Talapuru, Vinh Quach
- **Abstract**: Blockchain consensus mechanisms have relied on algorithms such as Proof-of-Work (PoW) and Proof-of-Stake (PoS) to ensure network functionality and integrity. However, these approaches struggle with adaptability for decision-making where the opinions of each matter rather than reaching an agreement based on honest majority or weighted consensus. This paper introduces a novel deliberation-based consensus mechanism where Large Language Models (LLMs) act as rational agents engaging in structured discussions to reach a unanimous consensus. By leveraging graded consensus and a multi-round deliberation process, our approach ensures both unanimous consensus for definitive problems and graded confidence for prioritized decisions and policies. We provide a formalization of our system and use it to show that the properties of blockchains: consistency, agreement, liveness, and determinism are maintained. Moreover, experimental results demonstrate our system's feasibility, showcasing how our deliberation method's convergence, block properties, and accuracy enable decision-making on blockchain networks. We also address key challenges with this novel approach such as degeneration of thoughts, hallucinations, malicious models and nodes, resource consumption, and scalability.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Achieving Unanimous Consensus in Decision Making Using Multi-Agents":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel deliberation-based consensus mechanism for blockchains using Large Language Models (LLMs) as rational agents.  Instead of traditional fault-tolerance approaches focusing on majority or weighted consensus, the authors propose a system where LLMs engage in structured discussions to reach *unanimous* consensus. The system utilizes graded consensus and a multi-round deliberation process to ensure both definitive answers for specific problems and graded confidence for prioritized decisions. The paper formalizes the system, proves its key blockchain properties (consistency, agreement, liveness, determinism), and presents experimental results demonstrating feasibility, convergence, and accuracy. It also addresses challenges like thought degeneration, hallucinations, malicious models, resource consumption, and scalability.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its *combination* of several existing concepts to address a specific problem. LLMs for deliberation and multi-agent systems are established fields. The idea of using deliberation for consensus in a *blockchain* setting is relatively new, especially with the goal of achieving *unanimous* consensus instead of fault tolerance. The specific architecture and prompt engineering techniques also contribute to the novelty. However, the individual components aren't groundbreaking on their own.
*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial limitation of existing blockchain consensus mechanisms: their inability to accommodate nuanced decision-making requiring consideration of every participant's viewpoint, something extremely important in applications like judicial systems, policy making, and constitution amendments. By achieving near unanimity in deliberation and decision-making the paper enhances trust and security in distributed networks.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Addresses a clear gap:**  The paper identifies a real limitation of current blockchain consensus mechanisms for certain important applications.
    *   **Well-structured and comprehensive:**  The paper provides a good overview of the related work, clearly defines the problem, and offers a comprehensive solution with formalization, implementation, and experimental results.
    *   **Addresses key challenges:** The paper acknowledges and begins to address critical challenges associated with using LLMs in this context, such as hallucinations and resource consumption.
    *   **Feasibility demonstrated:**  Experimental results, while preliminary, demonstrate the feasibility of the approach in a blockchain environment.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Experimental Validation:** The experimental results, while positive, are somewhat preliminary. More extensive testing on diverse and complex datasets is required to fully assess the system's scalability and robustness. The datasets used are either simplistic (arithmetic) or require manual annotation (wason card game, policy).
    *   **Scalability Concerns:** Achieving *unanimous* consensus in a large, decentralized network using LLMs is inherently resource-intensive and raises significant scalability concerns. The paper touches on this, but more detailed analysis and concrete solutions are needed.
    *   **Lack of comparison:** The paper does not explicitly compare its performance (accuracy, deliberation time, resource consumption) against existing approaches like smart contract based voting mechanisms in different voting scenarios.
    *   **Assumptions:**  The assumption of a 'strongly synchronized network' may not hold for all blockchain deployments. The reliance on an honest majority of nodes (as in traditional consensus) limits the scope. It does not adequately address the impact of adversarial attacks or malicious nodes on deliberation outcomes.
*   **Impact:** The paper has the potential to open new avenues for blockchain applications beyond simple financial transactions. If the scalability and robustness issues can be addressed, this approach could be valuable for building more democratic and inclusive decentralized systems. It provides a novel framework for exploring how LLMs can contribute to consensus mechanisms.

**Score: 6.5/10**

**Justification:**

The paper presents a worthwhile idea and a reasonably well-executed proof-of-concept for a new way to achieve consensus in blockchains. The combination of LLMs, multi-agent systems, and deliberation for *unanimous* decision-making addresses an important limitation of traditional consensus mechanisms. The formalization and initial experimental results support its feasibility.

However, the paper's relatively limited experimental validation and the significant scalability challenges inherent in the approach prevent a higher score. The work builds heavily on established concepts, thus decreasing the degree of novelty. The lack of comparison also undermines its significance. While the paper shows promise, further research is needed to demonstrate the practicality and robustness of the approach in real-world scenarios. If future work comprehensively addresses scalability, security, and robust evaluation, its significance and impact could increase considerably.

- **Classification**: cs.MA
- **Score**: 6/10

### On Simulation-Guided LLM-based Code Generation for Safe Autonomous Driving Software
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.02141v1)
- **Authors**: Ali Nouri, Johan Andersson, Kailash De Jesus Hornig, Zhennan Fei, Emil Knabe, Hakan Sivencrona, Beatriz Cabrero-Daniel, Christian Berger
- **Abstract**: Automated Driving System (ADS) is a safety-critical software system responsible for the interpretation of the vehicle's environment and making decisions accordingly. The unbounded complexity of the driving context, including unforeseeable events, necessitate continuous improvement, often achieved through iterative DevOps processes. However, DevOps processes are themselves complex, making these improvements both time- and resource-intensive. Automation in code generation for ADS using Large Language Models (LLM) is one potential approach to address this challenge. Nevertheless, the development of ADS requires rigorous processes to verify, validate, assess, and qualify the code before it can be deployed in the vehicle and used. In this study, we developed and evaluated a prototype for automatic code generation and assessment using a designed pipeline of a LLM-based agent, simulation model, and rule-based feedback generator in an industrial setup. The LLM-generated code is evaluated automatically in a simulation model against multiple critical traffic scenarios, and an assessment report is provided as feedback to the LLM for modification or bug fixing. We report about the experimental results of the prototype employing Codellama:34b, DeepSeek (r1:32b and Coder:33b), CodeGemma:7b, Mistral:7b, and GPT4 for Adaptive Cruise Control (ACC) and Unsupervised Collision Avoidance by Evasive Manoeuvre (CAEM). We finally assessed the tool with 11 experts at two Original Equipment Manufacturers (OEMs) by conducting an interview study.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the use of Large Language Models (LLMs) for automated code generation within the context of safety-critical Autonomous Driving Systems (ADS). It introduces a pipeline that leverages an LLM (specifically, GPT-4 and several open-source alternatives) to generate code for ADS functions, evaluates the generated code through simulation using esmini, and provides feedback to the LLM for iterative refinement. The pipeline automates the process of code generation, testing, and feedback, aiming to accelerate ADS function development. The study evaluates the performance of various LLM models using quantitative KPIs (number of passed test cases) and qualitative feedback from industry experts. It identifies limitations of LLMs for safety-related code generation and explores potential automated approaches to improve code quality. Finally, the paper conducts an interview study with industry experts to assess the practicality and usefulness of the proposed approach.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper makes a contribution by applying LLM-based code generation to a challenging and safety-critical domain (ADS). The focus on iterative refinement through simulation-based feedback is a key aspect that differentiates this work from simple code generation studies. The evaluation of several LLMs, including open-source alternatives, in the context of a complex industrial application is valuable.

*   **Significance:** ADS development is a complex and time-consuming process, and the potential for automation using LLMs is significant. This paper addresses a crucial need: ensuring safety and reliability in automatically generated ADS code. The use of simulation-based feedback and the integration with industry-standard tools like esmini adds to the practical significance of the work. The interview study further enhances its significance by providing insights into the perceived value and limitations from the perspective of industry experts.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Practical Relevance:** The research directly addresses a real-world problem in a safety-critical domain.
    *   **Iterative Feedback Loop:** The use of simulation-based feedback for code refinement is a strong approach.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The evaluation includes quantitative metrics, qualitative assessments from industry experts, and a comparison of multiple LLM models.
    *   **Identification of Limitations:** The paper identifies key limitations of LLMs for safety-related code generation, providing valuable insights for future research.
    *   **Detailed Methodology:** The methodology is well-defined and documented, increasing the reproducibility of the results.
    *   **Focus on Safety:** The work explicitly addresses safety requirements and tests the generated code against critical scenarios.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope of LLM use:** Most of the code generated can be generated with standard coding practices, and therefore the usefulness of LLM is reduced for most of the code.
    *   **Dependency on Simulation Fidelity:** The effectiveness of the feedback loop heavily depends on the accuracy and completeness of the simulation environment. The threat of construct validity needs to be emphasized to a greater degree.
    *   **Reliance on esmini:** The choice of esmini as the simulation environment might limit the generalizability of the findings to other ADS development environments. The connection to industry standard frameworks is questionable.
    *   **Limited Exploration of Prompt Engineering:** While the paper mentions prompt engineering, the specific techniques and their impact on code quality could be explored in more detail.
    *   **The paper does not provide statistical validation** for the results, and instead resorts to descriptions.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the direction of future research in LLM-based code generation for safety-critical systems. It highlights the importance of automated verification and validation techniques, the need for careful prompt engineering, and the challenges of using LLMs for complex, domain-specific tasks. The findings can also guide the development of more robust and reliable code generation tools for ADS. The identification of specific LLM limitations can inform the design of future training strategies and architectures.

**Score: 7**

**Justification:** The paper makes a valuable contribution to the field by applying LLM-based code generation to the safety-critical domain of ADS and by using a simulation-based feedback loop for iterative code refinement. The comprehensive evaluation and the identification of limitations add significant value. However, the dependence on the simulation environment and the limited scope of prompt engineering, in addition to the lack of statistical significance, weaken its overall impact. The novelty of using the simulation loop and testing on the software, alongside the evaluation through industrial experts, is a strength.

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 7/10

### LL4G: Self-Supervised Dynamic Optimization for Graph-Based Personality Detection
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.02146v1)
- **Authors**: Lingzhi Shen, Yunfei Long, Xiaohao Cai, Guanming Chen, Yuhan Wang, Imran Razzak, Shoaib Jameel
- **Abstract**: Graph-based personality detection constructs graph structures from textual data, particularly social media posts. Current methods often struggle with sparse or noisy data and rely on static graphs, limiting their ability to capture dynamic changes between nodes and relationships. This paper introduces LL4G, a self-supervised framework leveraging large language models (LLMs) to optimize graph neural networks (GNNs). LLMs extract rich semantic features to generate node representations and to infer explicit and implicit relationships. The graph structure adaptively adds nodes and edges based on input data, continuously optimizing itself. The GNN then uses these optimized representations for joint training on node reconstruction, edge prediction, and contrastive learning tasks. This integration of semantic and structural information generates robust personality profiles. Experimental results on Kaggle and Pandora datasets show LL4G outperforms state-of-the-art models.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LL4G, a self-supervised framework for personality detection using graph-based approaches. It leverages large language models (LLMs) to extract semantic features from text and construct dynamic graphs. These graphs are updated through self-supervised learning, addressing limitations of static graph approaches. A Graph Neural Network (GNN) performs representation learning on the dynamic graph, optimized through multi-task training (node reconstruction, edge prediction, contrastive learning). Experiments on Kaggle and Pandora datasets demonstrate LL4G's superior performance compared to state-of-the-art methods. The approach includes novelties such as dynamic graph optimization, explicit and implicit relational inference incorporating personalized and contradictory linguistic features, and a self-supervised mechanism to infer new nodes and edges.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Novel Approach:** LL4G presents a novel integration of LLMs and GNNs within a self-supervised framework for personality detection. The dynamic graph optimization is a significant departure from static graph methods.
*   **Comprehensive Design:** The framework incorporates personalized and contradictory linguistic features, which are often overlooked in traditional methods. The addition of a "user node" for holistic representation is a sound design choice.
*   **Strong Experimental Results:** LL4G achieves superior performance on benchmark datasets (Kaggle and Pandora) compared to state-of-the-art baselines. The ablation studies thoroughly evaluate the impact of individual components.
*   **Well-Defined Contributions:** The paper clearly articulates its contributions, including dynamic graph optimization and handling of feature sparsity and noise.
*  **Dynamic Adaptability:** Allows real-time graph updates as user data evolves, with a self-supervised mechanism inferring and completing new nodes and edges to address static graph limitations.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Complexity:** The framework involves multiple components (LLM, GNN, self-supervised tasks), increasing complexity in implementation and hyperparameter tuning.
*   **Computational Cost:**  Leveraging LLMs and dynamic graph updates can be computationally expensive, potentially limiting its applicability in resource-constrained environments. The paper doesn't thoroughly discuss computational cost and efficiency compared to baselines.
*   **Dataset Dependence:** While the framework performs well on Kaggle and Pandora, its generalization to other types of data (e.g., different social media platforms, conversational data) is not thoroughly evaluated.
*   **Limited Novelty in GNN Architecture:** While the overall framework is novel, the choice of D-DGCN as the GNN is fairly conventional. Further exploration of more advanced GNN architectures or custom GNN designs tailored to the dynamic graph structure could have been explored. The impact of incorporating user nodes is also an interesting concept to test.

**Significance:**

The paper's significance lies in its successful integration of LLMs and GNNs for a complex task (personality detection). The dynamic graph optimization approach offers a promising direction for handling sparse and noisy data, which is common in many real-world applications. The gains achieved over strong baselines demonstrate the potential of LL4G to advance the field.

**Novelty:**

The paper's novelty primarily stems from the dynamic graph optimization strategy in combination with self-supervision and incorporation of features such as personalized and contradictory linguistic traits within a LLM-GNN framework. While LLMs and GNNs have been used in isolation for personality detection, LL4G's unique architecture and self-supervised training paradigm is a novel contribution.

**Justification for Score:**

I assign a score of **7**. While the paper presents a well-designed and effective framework with strong experimental results, it has some limitations. The computational cost is not thoroughly addressed, and the novelty in the GNN component is somewhat limited. Despite the dynamic adaptability of the graph, the results may be skewed due to the initial graph construction, which may limit the effectiveness of the dynamic nature of graph optimization. Furthermore, while improvements are presented across various graph networks, such as GAT, it would be interesting to see a combined LL4G with GAT implementation, or to experiment with other dynamic graph optimization techniques to provide further novelty. However, the successful integration of LLMs and GNNs with a self-supervised dynamic optimization approach makes a solid contribution. The ability of LL4G to outperform prior state-of-the-art models is also a strong indication that the framework is well-designed.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### OmniCellTOSG: The First Cell Text-Omic Signaling Graphs Dataset for Joint LLM and GNN Modeling
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.02148v1)
- **Authors**: Heming Zhang, Tim Xu, Dekang Cao, Shunning Liang, Lars Schimmelpfennig, Levi Kaster, Di Huang, Carlos Cruchaga, Guangfu Li, Michael Province, Yixin Chen, Philip Payne, Fuhai Li
- **Abstract**: Complex cell signaling systems -- governed by varying protein abundances and interactions -- generate diverse cell types across organs. These systems evolve under influences such as age, sex, diet, environmental exposures, and diseases, making them challenging to decode given the involvement of tens of thousands of genes and proteins. Recently, hundreds of millions of single-cell omics data have provided a robust foundation for understanding these signaling networks within various cell subpopulations and conditions. Inspired by the success of large foundation models (for example, large language models and large vision models) pre-trained on massive datasets, we introduce OmniCellTOSG, the first dataset of cell text-omic signaling graphs (TOSGs). Each TOSG represents the signaling network of an individual or meta-cell and is labeled with information such as organ, disease, sex, age, and cell subtype. OmniCellTOSG offers two key contributions. First, it introduces a novel graph model that integrates human-readable annotations -- such as biological functions, cellular locations, signaling pathways, related diseases, and drugs -- with quantitative gene and protein abundance data, enabling graph reasoning to decode cell signaling. This approach calls for new joint models combining large language models and graph neural networks. Second, the dataset is built from single-cell RNA sequencing data of approximately 120 million cells from diverse tissues and conditions (healthy and diseased) and is fully compatible with PyTorch. This facilitates the development of innovative cell signaling models that could transform research in life sciences, healthcare, and precision medicine. The OmniCellTOSG dataset is continuously expanding and will be updated regularly. The dataset and code are available at https://github.com/FuhaiLiAiLab/OmniCellTOSG.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the "OmniCellTOSG: The First Cell Text-Omic Signaling Graphs Dataset for Joint LLM and GNN Modeling" paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces OmniCellTOSG, a novel dataset designed to facilitate the joint modeling of cell signaling systems using large language models (LLMs) and graph neural networks (GNNs). This dataset combines single-cell transcriptomic data from diverse sources (CellxGene, GEO, Brain Cell Atlas, SEA-AD) with textual annotations derived from BioMedGraphica, providing both numerical omic features and human-understandable knowledge (biological functions, cellular locations, diseases, etc.) for individual cells or meta-cells. The authors detail the data collection, preprocessing, and graph generation procedures. They also discuss the potential for this dataset to enable the development of new foundation models capable of decoding complex cell signaling systems, and provide an API to access the data.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper's primary contribution is the creation and release of a new dataset. The idea of combining text and omic data for cell signaling analysis is not entirely new, but the scale and structure of OmniCellTOSG, specifically tailored for joint LLM and GNN modeling, represent a significant advancement. Combining massive cell data with biomedical textual information in graph structure is valuable for the data mining and biomedical field.

**Significance:** The potential significance of OmniCellTOSG lies in its ability to drive the development of more sophisticated AI models for understanding cell signaling. Traditional approaches using either omic data alone or manually curated pathways often fall short in capturing the complexity of cellular processes. By integrating these two modalities, the authors aim to enable models to reason about cell signaling in a more comprehensive and human-interpretable way. The dataset could potentially lead to breakthroughs in disease pathogenesis understanding, drug target discovery, and precision medicine.

**Strengths:**

*   **Scale and Diversity:** The dataset's size (initially over 100 million cells, reduced to half a million carefully processed meta-cells) and the diversity of its sources (various tissues, diseases, and cell types) are major strengths. This scale is necessary for training effective foundation models.
*   **Integration of Text and Omics:** The combination of omic data with textual annotations is a key innovation that addresses a major limitation of existing approaches. Text data facilitates the interpretability and reasoning capabilities of AI models.
*   **Systematic Data Processing:** The authors have taken care to ensure cross-dataset compatibility through rigorous preprocessing, normalization, and cell type annotation refinement.
*   **API and Open Access:**  The provision of a Python API and open access to the dataset will facilitate its adoption by the research community.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Reliance on Existing Knowledge:** While the integration of textual annotations is a strength, the dataset's reliance on existing knowledge (from BioMedGraphica) also means that it may be limited by the biases and incompleteness of that knowledge. The ability of the dataset to facilitate discovery of *new* knowledge is therefore somewhat constrained.
*   **Complexity of Graph Generation:** The graph generation process, while described in detail, is complex and may require significant effort for researchers to fully understand and utilize.
*   **Limited Experimental Validation:** The paper presents some preliminary results of applying the dataset to cell type classification, but more extensive experimental validation is needed to demonstrate its full potential.
*   **Dataset Updates** : Dataset sizes are already small when focusing on a specific organ/disease which limits how good a foundation model can be trained. The impact is contingent on dataset growth and ongoing updates.

**Potential Impact:**

OmniCellTOSG could be a valuable resource for researchers developing and evaluating new AI models for cell signaling analysis. It has the potential to drive advances in our understanding of disease pathogenesis and lead to the development of more effective therapies. The combination of LLMs and GNNs on this kind of dataset is an active research area, and this dataset could accelerate progress.

**Justification for Score:**

While the paper presents a novel dataset and provides a framework for joint text-omic analysis, its current impact on the field is primarily potential. The challenges involved in working with such a complex dataset, the reliance on existing knowledge, and the limited experimental validation warrant a conservative assessment. The dataset is a first of its kind and unlocks new opportunities to analyze single cell omic data, which is valuable. Thus, given the data curation and preprocessing efforts, and the new research opportunities from joining data, OmniCellTOSG is a good contribution to the biomedical field.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### FreSca: Unveiling the Scaling Space in Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.02154v1)
- **Authors**: Chao Huang, Susan Liang, Yunlong Tang, Li Ma, Yapeng Tian, Chenliang Xu
- **Abstract**: Diffusion models offer impressive controllability for image tasks, primarily through noise predictions that encode task-specific information and classifier-free guidance enabling adjustable scaling. This scaling mechanism implicitly defines a ``scaling space'' whose potential for fine-grained semantic manipulation remains underexplored. We investigate this space, starting with inversion-based editing where the difference between conditional/unconditional noise predictions carries key semantic information. Our core contribution stems from a Fourier analysis of noise predictions, revealing that its low- and high-frequency components evolve differently throughout diffusion. Based on this insight, we introduce FreSca, a straightforward method that applies guidance scaling independently to different frequency bands in the Fourier domain. FreSca demonstrably enhances existing image editing methods without retraining. Excitingly, its effectiveness extends to image understanding tasks such as depth estimation, yielding quantitative gains across multiple datasets.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "FreSca: Unveiling the Scaling Space in Diffusion Models":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces FreSca, a novel approach to enhance diffusion models by manipulating the scaling space in the Fourier domain.  The authors observe that the scaling space, traditionally controlled by a single scalar, implicitly mixes low- and high-frequency components. They propose decomposing the noise prediction difference (Δe) into low- and high-frequency components and scaling them independently. This allows for finer-grained control over image editing, enabling better structural fidelity and detail preservation.  The method is presented as a plug-and-play enhancement that can be integrated into existing diffusion model architectures without retraining.  The authors demonstrate the effectiveness of FreSca in image editing tasks and also extend it to image understanding tasks, specifically depth estimation, showing quantitative improvements.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The core idea of frequency-aware scaling within diffusion models is a valuable insight. While some prior works have explored manipulating diffusion models at test time, FreSca's systematic analysis of the low- and high-frequency dynamics and its straightforward implementation are novel. The decomposition of the scaling space and the ability to control these components independently represent a significant advancement. This is beyond simply manipulating existing architectural components, such as the skip connections as done by FreeU.
*   **Significance:** The significance of FreSca lies in its ability to improve existing diffusion-based methods without requiring retraining or substantial architectural modifications. The plug-and-play nature makes it easily adaptable to a wide range of tasks, including image editing and depth estimation.  The demonstrated quantitative improvements in depth estimation are particularly impactful, suggesting the broader applicability of FreSca. Moreover, the analysis of the scaling space sheds light on the inner workings of diffusion models, providing a better understanding of their behavior. The findings could spur further research into more sophisticated control mechanisms.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear and well-motivated:**  The paper clearly explains the motivation behind FreSca, building upon a solid understanding of diffusion model mechanics.
    *   **Simple and effective:**  The proposed method is straightforward to implement and computationally efficient, requiring only a few lines of code.
    *   **Generalizable:** FreSca is not limited to a specific diffusion model architecture or task, demonstrating its versatility. The demonstrated extension to both image editing and depth estimation highlights its broad applicability.
    *   **Quantitative and Qualitative Validation:** The paper provides both quantitative and qualitative results to support its claims, bolstering confidence in the effectiveness of FreSca.
    *   **Thorough ablation studies:** The inclusion of ablation studies provides valuable insights into the contribution of each component of FreSca.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope of Tasks:** While promising, the method is demonstrated on a few key tasks (image editing, depth estimation). More diverse tasks could further strengthen its generalizability claim.
    *   **Parameter Sensitivity:** Although the implementation is plug-and-play, some parameter tuning (h) is still required, especially across different methods/architectures. The method’s success relies on correctly identifying and utilizing an appropriate 'h' parameter.
    *   **Potential for Optimizing Frequency-Aware Decomposition:** The current implementation relies on a simple frequency split using a fixed threshold. More sophisticated frequency decomposition techniques (e.g., wavelet transform, adaptive filters) could potentially lead to further improvements.

**Overall:**

FreSca offers a valuable contribution to the field by providing a novel and effective way to control the scaling space in diffusion models. The method's simplicity, generalizability, and demonstrated improvements in both image editing and image understanding tasks make it a significant advancement.  The weaknesses are primarily related to opportunities for future research and refinement, rather than fundamental flaws in the approach.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Less-to-More Generalization: Unlocking More Controllability by In-Context Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.02160v1)
- **Authors**: Shaojin Wu, Mengqi Huang, Wenxu Wu, Yufeng Cheng, Fei Ding, Qian He
- **Abstract**: Although subject-driven generation has been extensively explored in image generation due to its wide applications, it still has challenges in data scalability and subject expansibility. For the first challenge, moving from curating single-subject datasets to multiple-subject ones and scaling them is particularly difficult. For the second, most recent methods center on single-subject generation, making it hard to apply when dealing with multi-subject scenarios. In this study, we propose a highly-consistent data synthesis pipeline to tackle this challenge. This pipeline harnesses the intrinsic in-context generation capabilities of diffusion transformers and generates high-consistency multi-subject paired data. Additionally, we introduce UNO, which consists of progressive cross-modal alignment and universal rotary position embedding. It is a multi-image conditioned subject-to-image model iteratively trained from a text-to-image model. Extensive experiments show that our method can achieve high consistency while ensuring controllability in both single-subject and multi-subject driven generation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Less-to-More Generalization: Unlocking More Controllability by In-Context Generation":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenges of data scalability and subject expandability in subject-driven image generation. It proposes a model-data co-evolution paradigm where less-controllable models generate data to train more controllable subsequent models. Specifically, it introduces UNO, a multi-image conditioned subject-to-image model, trained iteratively from a text-to-image model using synthetically generated multi-subject paired data. A progressive synthesis pipeline creates high-consistency multi-subject paired data, and universal rotary position embedding (UnoPE) mitigates attribute confusion when scaling visual subject controls. The authors demonstrate that UNO achieves high consistency and controllability in both single-subject and multi-subject driven generation tasks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper identifies a crucial limitation in subject-driven image generation: the bottleneck of data acquisition and the difficulties in scaling to multi-subject scenarios.
    *   The proposed model-data co-evolution paradigm is a conceptually interesting and potentially impactful approach. The idea of using synthetic data to train more capable models can circumvent the limitations of real-world datasets. This idea leverages the strength of large language model to create data systematically.
    *   The technical contributions appear solid. The progressive synthesis pipeline and UnoPE seem to address specific challenges in multi-subject generation, such as consistency and attribute confusion. The iterative training process seems well-designed.
    *   The experimental results show promising performance on standard benchmarks, surpassing existing methods in DINO and CLIP-I scores, indicating improved subject similarity and text controllability. User studies seem to support those findings as well.
    *   The applications of UNO to various multi-image conditional scenarios are interesting.
    *   The supplementary material provides detailed information on the dataset creation and experimental setup, enhancing reproducibility.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The reliance on synthetic data, while innovative, raises questions about the realism and potential biases introduced by the initial T2I model. The quality of the final generated images is ultimately dependent on the quality of the synthetic data.
    *   While the authors address the copy-paste issue, there is potential for the model to learn to reproduce artifacts or biases present in the synthetic data. More thorough analysis is needed.
    *   The improvements in CLIP-T score is still limited, potentially indicating limited capability to follow the detail edit instructions.
    *   The presentation could be clearer in places. The paper introduces multiple components and acronyms (UnoPE, S2I, etc.) which takes time to digest.

*   **Novelty:**

    *   The model-data co-evolution paradigm is a relatively novel idea in the context of image generation, drawing inspiration from LLM research.
    *   The application of DiT and related modifications for generating multi-subject image.

*   **Significance:**
    *   If the method proves robust and generalizable, it could significantly impact various applications such as content creation, virtual try-on, and industrial design.
    *   The work suggests a pathway towards developing more scalable and controllable image generation models by leveraging the capabilities of synthetic data and progressive training.
    *   Addressing the limitations of existing dataset is important and will benefit future image generation research.

*   **Impact and Potential Influence:**
    *   The model-data co-evolution paradigm could inspire further research into leveraging synthetic data for training generative models.
    *   The specific technical contributions of the paper, such as the progressive synthesis pipeline and UnoPE, may be adopted and extended by other researchers in the field.
    *   The focus on multi-subject driven generation addresses an increasingly important use case, potentially influencing the direction of future research.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a solid contribution to subject-driven image generation by addressing a key limitation (data scalability) and proposing an innovative solution. The combination of synthetic data, progressive training, and specialized architectural modifications (UnoPE) demonstrates improvements in both subject similarity and text controllability. While the reliance on synthetic data raises concerns about realism and potential biases, the results presented are compelling. A score of 7 reflects the novelty of the proposed co-evolution approach, the significance of the problem addressed, the solid technical contributions, and the promising experimental results, tempered by the limitations of the synthetic data and the need for further analysis of potential biases.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Responsible Innovation: A Strategic Framework for Financial LLM Integration
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.02165v1)
- **Authors**: Ahmadreza Tavasoli, Maedeh Sharbaf, Seyed Mohamad Madani
- **Abstract**: Financial institutions of all sizes are increasingly adopting Large Language Models (LLMs) to enhance credit assessments, deliver personalized client advisory services, and automate various language-intensive processes. However, effectively deploying LLMs requires careful management of stringent data governance requirements, heightened demands for interpretability, ethical responsibilities, and rapidly evolving regulatory landscapes. To address these challenges, we introduce a structured six-decision framework specifically designed for the financial sector, guiding organizations systematically from initial feasibility assessments to final deployment strategies. The framework encourages institutions to: (1) evaluate whether an advanced LLM is necessary at all, (2) formalize robust data governance and privacy safeguards, (3) establish targeted risk management mechanisms, (4) integrate ethical considerations early in the development process, (5) justify the initiative's return on investment (ROI) and strategic value, and only then (6) choose the optimal implementation pathway -- open-source versus proprietary, or in-house versus vendor-supported -- aligned with regulatory requirements and operational realities. By linking strategic considerations with practical steps such as pilot testing, maintaining comprehensive audit trails, and conducting ongoing compliance evaluations, this decision framework offers a structured roadmap for responsibly leveraging LLMs. Rather than acting as a rigid, one-size-fits-all solution, it shows how advanced language models can be thoughtfully integrated into existing workflows -- balancing innovation with accountability to uphold stakeholder trust and regulatory integrity.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenges and strategic considerations involved in integrating Large Language Models (LLMs) into the financial sector. It proposes a six-decision framework to guide financial institutions from initial feasibility assessments to deployment strategies. The framework covers evaluating the necessity of LLMs, data governance, risk management, ethical considerations, ROI justification, and selecting the optimal implementation pathway. The paper emphasizes responsible innovation, balancing technological advancements with compliance, ethical integrity, and sustainable strategic value. The framework is presented as a practical roadmap to integrate LLMs into existing workflows, promoting accountability and stakeholder trust.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** While the individual components of the framework (data governance, risk management, ethical considerations, etc.) are not novel in isolation, the paper's *integrated* approach and *specific tailoring to the financial sector* represent a useful contribution. The paper synthesizes existing concerns and best practices into a cohesive framework. It's not a groundbreaking theoretical advancement, but it offers a practical guide, which is a valuable addition to the growing literature on LLMs in finance. The paper explicitly tackles bridging the gap between high-level principles and actionable steps, which is often missing in theoretical discussions on AI ethics and governance.

* **Significance:** The paper addresses a significant problem. The financial industry is rapidly adopting LLMs, but often without adequate consideration for the associated risks and ethical implications. The framework provides a structured way to think about LLM deployment, promoting responsible innovation and mitigating potential harm. The emphasis on early feasibility checks (is an LLM *really* needed?), data governance and the continuous monitoring are particularly crucial for financial institutions. It can help prevent misapplication and misuse of LLMs, leading to better outcomes for stakeholders. The framework's scalability across different organization sizes (from global banks to fintech startups) further enhances its potential impact. The discussion around balancing proprietary vs. open-source and in-house vs. vendor solutions, with explicit considerations for data security and compliance is very useful.

* **Strengths:**
    *   **Practical Focus:** The framework provides actionable steps and considerations for financial institutions.
    *   **Comprehensive Coverage:** It addresses a wide range of issues, from data governance and risk management to ethical implications and ROI.
    *   **Specific Tailoring:** The framework is specifically designed for the financial sector, considering its unique regulatory and ethical constraints.
    *   **Clear Structure:** The six-decision framework provides a clear and logical roadmap for LLM implementation.
    *   **Visual Aids:** The flowcharts and tables (decision flowchart, KPI matrix, comparison of adaptation methods, and operational safeguards) enhance understanding.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Empirical Validation:** The paper presents the framework but does not offer real-world case studies or empirical evidence to support its effectiveness. Future work could benefit from implementing the framework in a financial institution and evaluating its impact.
    *   **Lack of Quantitative Metrics:** While the paper discusses ROI, it doesn't provide specific quantitative metrics or methodologies for measuring the success of LLM deployments.
    *   **Potential for Oversimplification:** A framework with six decisions necessarily abstracts away some of the complexities involved in LLM implementation.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the way financial institutions approach LLM adoption, promoting responsible innovation and mitigating potential risks. It can also serve as a starting point for future research and development in this area. The framework provides a common language and structure for discussing LLM integration within the financial industry, facilitating collaboration and knowledge sharing.

*   **Rigorous Rationale:** This paper is essentially a *call to action* (and a framework to follow) based on current best practices and emerging regulatory concerns, which makes its integration of key ideas very valuable, but also places it a bit lower on the *novelty* scale. It addresses a real and pressing problem, and its strengths outweigh its weaknesses, but the lack of empirical validation at this stage does limit its influence.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CE
- **Score**: 7/10

### MDP: Multidimensional Vision Model Pruning with Latency Constraint
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.02168v1)
- **Authors**: Xinglong Sun, Barath Lakshmanan, Maying Shen, Shiyi Lan, Jingde Chen, Jose M. Alvarez
- **Abstract**: Current structural pruning methods face two significant limitations: (i) they often limit pruning to finer-grained levels like channels, making aggressive parameter reduction challenging, and (ii) they focus heavily on parameter and FLOP reduction, with existing latency-aware methods frequently relying on simplistic, suboptimal linear models that fail to generalize well to transformers, where multiple interacting dimensions impact latency. In this paper, we address both limitations by introducing Multi-Dimensional Pruning (MDP), a novel paradigm that jointly optimizes across a variety of pruning granularities-including channels, query, key, heads, embeddings, and blocks. MDP employs an advanced latency modeling technique to accurately capture latency variations across all prunable dimensions, achieving an optimal balance between latency and accuracy. By reformulating pruning as a Mixed-Integer Nonlinear Program (MINLP), MDP efficiently identifies the optimal pruned structure across all prunable dimensions while respecting latency constraints. This versatile framework supports both CNNs and transformers. Extensive experiments demonstrate that MDP significantly outperforms previous methods, especially at high pruning ratios. On ImageNet, MDP achieves a 28% speed increase with a +1.4 Top-1 accuracy improvement over prior work like HALP for ResNet50 pruning. Against the latest transformer pruning method, Isomorphic, MDP delivers an additional 37% acceleration with a +0.7 Top-1 accuracy improvement.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Multi-Dimensional Pruning (MDP), a novel structural pruning paradigm for deep neural networks that aims to overcome limitations of existing methods. Specifically, MDP jointly optimizes across various pruning granularities (channels, query/key, heads, embeddings, blocks) and employs an advanced latency modeling technique to accurately capture latency variations across all prunable dimensions. The pruning problem is reformulated as a Mixed-Integer Nonlinear Program (MINLP), which allows for identifying the optimal pruned structure while respecting latency constraints. The framework supports both CNNs and transformers and is shown to outperform previous methods, especially at high pruning ratios, on tasks ranging from ImageNet classification to NuScenes 3D detection.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the multi-dimensional approach to structural pruning, accurately modelling latency across various dimensions with the MINLP formulation. Existing methods often focus on a single or a limited number of granularities or employ simplified latency models. Integrating channel, head, embedding, and block pruning within a joint optimization is a strong contribution. The formulation as MINLP to directly solve the optimisation problem while adhering to latency constraints also adds novelty.

*   **Significance:**  The potential significance is high because the paper addresses a practical problem: deploying large models on resource-constrained devices while maintaining accuracy. The ability to achieve high pruning ratios without significant accuracy loss has practical implications for real-world applications. The demonstrated improvements on ImageNet and NuScenes datasets are compelling. The ability to handle transformers more effectively than prior latency aware methods gives this technique a distinct advantage.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Comprehensive experimental evaluation across CNNs and transformers.
    *   Strong performance compared to state-of-the-art pruning methods, especially at high pruning ratios.
    *   Addresses key limitations of existing methods related to granularity and latency modeling.
    *   Clear and well-written presentation.
    *   The code release increases reproducibility.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The MINLP formulation, while powerful, might have limitations in scalability to extremely large models. While the paper says the problem is tractable, further study could be necessary for very large transformers or networks.
    *   While latency LUT is good, its hardware dependency makes the entire process a bit cumbersome. An architecture agnostic hardware modelling can be advantageous.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to influence future research in structural pruning, particularly in the development of more sophisticated and accurate latency models. The MINLP formulation could inspire other optimization-based pruning methods.

*   **Justification of Score:** The paper presents a well-executed and novel approach to a practically important problem. The strong experimental results and clear presentation contribute to its overall impact. While some limitations exist regarding scalability and hardware dependency, the strengths outweigh these concerns.

**Score: 8.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Subasa -- Adapting Language Models for Low-resourced Offensive Language Detection in Sinhala
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.02178v1)
- **Authors**: Shanilka Haturusinghe, Tharindu Cyril Weerasooriya, Marcos Zampieri, Christopher M. Homan, S. R. Liyanage
- **Abstract**: Accurate detection of offensive language is essential for a number of applications related to social media safety. There is a sharp contrast in performance in this task between low and high-resource languages. In this paper, we adapt fine-tuning strategies that have not been previously explored for Sinhala in the downstream task of offensive language detection. Using this approach, we introduce four models: "Subasa-XLM-R", which incorporates an intermediate Pre-Finetuning step using Masked Rationale Prediction. Two variants of "Subasa-Llama" and "Subasa-Mistral", are fine-tuned versions of Llama (3.2) and Mistral (v0.3), respectively, with a task-specific strategy. We evaluate our models on the SOLD benchmark dataset for Sinhala offensive language detection. All our models outperform existing baselines. Subasa-XLM-R achieves the highest Macro F1 score (0.84) surpassing state-of-the-art large language models like GPT-4o when evaluated on the same SOLD benchmark dataset under zero-shot settings. The models and code are publicly available.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of offensive language detection in Sinhala, a low-resource language. The authors introduce four novel models: Subasa-XLM-R, Subasa-Llama (two variants), and Subasa-Mistral. Subasa-XLM-R utilizes intermediate pre-finetuning with Masked Rationale Prediction (MRP), while Subasa-Llama and Subasa-Mistral are task-specifically fine-tuned using QLoRA (4-bit quantization). The models are evaluated on the SOLD dataset, and the results show significant improvements over existing baselines and even outperform large language models like GPT-4o (in zero-shot setting). The paper explores different fine-tuning strategies and provides ablation studies to understand the impact of each component.  The code and models are made publicly available.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Addresses a significant gap:** The paper tackles a critical issue in NLP – the under-representation of low-resource languages in offensive language detection. Sinhala is a particularly relevant case study given its real-world impact on social unrest.
    *   **Novel Application of Techniques:**  The application of intermediate pre-finetuning techniques like MRP to Sinhala is novel and shows promising results. The use of LoRA with quantization allows effective adaptation of larger LLMs.
    *   **Comprehensive Experiments and Analysis:** The paper includes thorough ablation studies, exploring different masking ratios and intermediate tasks. This helps to understand the contributions of each component.
    *   **Strong Empirical Results:** The results convincingly demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed models, outperforming baselines and even GPT-4o in a zero-shot setting. The fact that even smaller finetuned models surpass larger zero-shot models is significant.
    *   **Publicly Available Resources:** The availability of the code and models will enable further research and development in Sinhala NLP.
    *  **Relevant Baselines:** The chosen baselines are appropriate, including both traditional methods (CNNs) and PLM-based approaches (XLM-R).  The inclusion of Aya101 and GPT-40 as zero-shot baselines further strengthens the comparison.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited scope of the fine-tuning strategy:** The paper focuses on a single prompt template during the task-specific fine-tuning.  Exploring multiple prompt designs would have provided a more robust evaluation.
    *   **Reliance on a single dataset:** The evaluation is limited to the SOLD dataset. Evaluating on other Sinhala datasets (if available) or creating new ones would increase the generalizability of the findings.
    *   **XLM-R limited variant:** The hardware limitations of their study prevented them from using XLM-R large, which could have affected the results of the best performance their model could achieve.
    *   **Zero-shot vs. Finetuned:** The comparison against GPT-40 is based on zero-shot results.  It would be interesting to see how GPT-40 performs with task-specific finetuning, even if it is more computationally expensive. This provides a ceiling of how far the current methods can go.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    *   The novelty lies in the successful adaptation and application of MRP and QLoRA-based fine-tuning strategies to Sinhala offensive language detection.  While the individual techniques are not entirely novel, their combination and application to this specific low-resource language contribute to the field.
    *   The significance is considerable, as it provides a valuable resource and methodology for improving NLP capabilities for Sinhala, particularly in an area critical for social media safety. The findings also suggest that these techniques could be beneficial for other low-resource languages. The study also opens up avenues for further research on intermediate tasks and the transfer of knowledge from high-resource to low-resource settings.

**Overall Assessment:**

The paper makes a solid contribution to the field by addressing a significant challenge in NLP and providing effective solutions for a low-resource language. The experiments are well-designed, and the results are convincing. The release of code and models will facilitate further research. The main weaknesses are the limited scope of the prompt engineering and reliance on a single dataset for evaluation. Taking into account the novel application of the techniques and significant empirical improvements, I believe a strong score is justified.

**Score: 7.5**
